The curve is then drawn only on that interval, with a filled dot at a closed end and a hollow dot at an open end; every requiredPoint must lie in the interval and none on an open end.Preserve all source-required points/tangency anchors/intercepts as requiredPoints:[{id:string,x:number,y:number}]; these are independently reviewed source/solution features, never convenient invented points. The main overview must show the vertex and enough of both arms to make the opening and symmetry legible. A local detail cannot replace the overview. Do not guess symbolic coefficients. Reject unsupported source. caption is short student-facing Korean, with no internal/debug terms. Either plan must have sourceConditions:[{id:string,condition:string,mappedTo:string|string[]}]; mappedTo names one or more targets in the plan or PRESERVED_ARCHIVE_BANK. Include newVisualInformation:[string] and no unsupported source condition. No SVG, raw derived coordinates, or measurements. Also supported: SEGMENT_LENGTH(inputs two POINT, args{}, outputType SCALAR), SCALAR_SQUARE(inputs one SCALAR,args{},outputType SCALAR), and SCALAR_RATIO(inputs two SCALAR,args{},outputType SCALAR). Supported bounded construction operations: PARALLEL_THROUGH(inputs POINT,LINE), ANGLE_BISECTOR(inputs ordered points [A,V,B], args {mode:INTERNAL|EXTERNAL}), CIRCLE_THROUGH_3(inputs three non-collinear POINT), CIRCLE_THROUGH_POINT(inputs center,boundary POINT), TANGENT_AT_POINT(inputs CIRCLE,POINT on the circle), and LINE_INTERSECTION(inputs two nonparallel LINE) for a unique point. Use INTERSECTION plus SELECT_POINT for multiple circle-line/circle-circle roots and declare the oriented source branch. mathPlan.conditionAudits may contain only typed checks with exact refs and a required sourceConditionId that matches one top-level sourceConditions.id: INCIDENCE_POINT_ON_LINE(POINT,LINE), DISTANCE_EQUALS(POINT,POINT,expected:typedScalar), EQUAL_DISTANCE(POINT,POINT,POINT,POINT), MIDPOINT_RATIO(start,middle,end,expected:typedScalar), PERPENDICULAR(LINE,LINE), PARALLEL(LINE,LINE), CIRCLE_MEMBERSHIP(CIRCLE,POINT), TANGENCY_AT_POINT(CIRCLE,POINT,LINE), ORIENTED_SIDE(start,end,point,sign:+1|-1). Meanings: DISTANCE_EQUALS expected is the exact length |PQ|; EQUAL_DISTANCE means |PQ|=|RS|; MIDPOINT_RATIO requires middle on segment start-end and expected is the ratio of the two parts |start-middle| divided by |middle-end|, never a part divided by the whole (a midpoint has expected 1; the centroid on a median from vertex C to midpoint M has refs C,G,M and expected 2); CIRCLE_MEMBERSHIP means the point is on the circle; TANGENCY_AT_POINT means the point is on the circle and on the line and the line is perpendicular to the radius there. Each check is exactly {id,kind,refs,sourceConditionId} plus expected or sign where listed, with a unique id. Every check must bind to a stated source condition; do not invent coordinates or substitute general constraint solving. Geometry must calculate decisive lengths and relations using nodes when relevant; scalarLabels:[{nodeId,ownerSegment,prefix,unit?:string}] displays exact results near the owner segment. Also supported rightAngles:[{id,refs:[rayPoint,vertex,rayPoint],factRole:GIVEN|DERIVED_INTERMEDIATE}] and segmentLabels:[{id,ownerSegment,variable:single-letter,unit?:cm|m,factRole:GIVEN}]. Do not create duplicate labels with the same owner and content. Avoid unnecessary construction nodes when a display segment suffices. All scalar node dependencies may follow SELECT_POINT. Display segments use IDs distinct from mathPlan point/scalar IDs. Original question content, score, choices, answer and solution remain unchanged in the actual Archive bank; map these conditions to PRESERVED_ARCHIVE_BANK. IF AND ONLY IF the source visual is a polynomial graph of exact degree 3 or 4, apply this separate bounded contract; otherwise ignore this clause and follow the existing geometry/quadratic rules. Such cubic/quartic graph coefficients must have exact degree 3 or 4 and sourceDomain must be exactly {kind:'ALL_REALS'}; restricted intervals are unsupported in this slice. Do not apply quadratic vertex framing to these graphs. The overview framer derives and binds all real roots, derivative roots (extrema or stationary inflections), inflection points, multiplicities, and left/right tail directions from the exact coefficient strings. The independent observer must recompute this inventory from the source polynomial and check every feature and tail against the final rendered polyline. Repeated real roots keep their exact multiplicity: a double (even multiplicity) root is a tangency point where the curve touches the x-axis and stays on one side, a triple root crosses the axis with a flattening; overviewFeatures records the root multiplicity, and the observer recomputes it from the source polynomial and checks the final rendered polyline (it reaches the axis at each root, stays on one side at an even root, changes side at an odd root, and a multiplicity-3 root keeps a readable flat band). A repeated root of degree 3 or 4 is a root of a rational quadratic factor (rational or p+q*sqrt(r)), so it is representable; a repeat whose isolation cannot be pinned down is refused with UNSUPPORTED_CUBIC_QUARTIC_REPEATED_ROOT_IMPRECISE. Root/feature separations below one actual CSS pixel at the audited profile are unsupported (the observer returns UNSUPPORTED with OVERVIEW_ROOTS_BELOW_DISPLAY_RESOLUTION or OVERVIEW_FEATURES_BELOW_DISPLAY_RESOLUTION). For degree 3 or 4 the formula header writes the polynomial with stacked fractions (x^3-3/4*x-1/4), never parentheses around a fraction. Do not invent source-required points; preserve those separately in requiredPoints. In an explicitly supplied coordinate plane, NORMALIZATION_ORIGIN and unit NORMALIZATION_AXIS may be coordinate-axis references: declare mathPlan.realization:{recipeId:"SOURCE_COORDINATE_AXES_v1",unit:"source-coordinate",reflectionEquivalent:false}; axis length must be exactly 1. Retain every SOURCE_POINT in its original coordinate frame. This is not SSS normalization or a license to invent derived coordinates. The mathematical DAG may contain internal axis/projection helpers. Explicit optional displayPoints:[point IDs], displayLines:[line IDs] and displayCircles:[circle IDs] select the final educational drawing (a displayed point is written with its node id, so use the names the stored solution uses as node ids, for example A, B, M, P, and never ids such as O0, Pp or C1 that the solution does not use; helper points the solution does not name should be DERIVED_INTERMEDIATE and listed in hiddenPointNames); they are top-level plan fields, never inside mathPlan, and when one is omitted every node of that type is drawn (circles made by CIRCLE_CENTER_RADIUS are drawn as geometry). Each list must retain all original SOURCE_POINT nodes, GIVEN/CONCLUSION geometry of its type and every annotation/segment endpoint. Optional top-level coordinatePlacement:"PANEL" keeps only the point names at the points and lists each coordinateLabels point as A(x,y) in a band above the figure; use it when several labelled points would crowd the figure, or set coordinatePanel:[point IDs] to move only those points; labelDirections:{pointId:"SE"} names the first direction (N, NE, E, SE, S, SW, W or NW) tried for a coordinate label that stays at its point. A displayed line that is the x-axis or the y-axis is not drawn again (the axis shows it); do not list it in displayLines. Optional top-level hiddenPointNames:[point IDs] draws derived helper points (DERIVED_INTERMEDIATE, not in coordinateLabels) as dots without a name; use it for helpers the solution never names. A PLANE_FIGURE_v1 plan may also list a SOURCE_POINT the source figure never names (it is drawn without a name; pointNames, coordinateLabels or a caption or label text that names it are refused with HIDDEN_POINT_NAMED_IN_TEXT), and the optional top-level hiddenPointStyle:"NONE" (default "DOT", a plane figure with hidden points only) then draws the hidden points with no dot at all. For a quadratic graph, coordinatePanel:[requiredPoint IDs] or coordinatePlacement:"PANEL" names those integer key points by their id (one capital letter, optionally a digit or prime) and lists P(x,y) under the formula. For the x-axis equidistance construction, show the source A,B and derived P, segments PA and PB and their equal lengths; axis/projection helper points and auxiliary lines may stay in the exact DAG without cluttering the drawing. Use short mathematical point IDs, and use prefix:"" for a pure length value without an equation prefix; any other prefix is one or two Latin letters, optionally followed by ^2 or ^3 (for example AB, r, S^2). A construction plan may set axisTickValues:{x:[numbers],y:[numbers]} to choose the axis ticks (a polynomial graphPlan always uses {x:[],y:[]}); use {x:[],y:[]} when every displayed key point already carries a coordinate label, and never put a tick at a value whose coordinate label is drawn next to that axis. Also supported point/line construction operations: DIVISION_POINT(inputs [A,B] two different POINT, args {m:typedScalar,n:typedScalar,mode:INTERNAL|EXTERNAL}, outputType POINT; the point P with AP:PB=m:n, m and n positive numbers; INTERNAL lies between A and B, EXTERNAL needs m different from n and lies beyond B when m>n and beyond A when m<n), CENTROID(inputs three non-collinear POINT vertices, args {}, outputType POINT), POINT_REFLECTION(inputs [P,centre] POINT, args {}, outputType POINT; the image of P in the point centre, which must differ from P, and for a reflection in the origin the centre is the NORMALIZATION_ORIGIN node), LINE_REFLECTION(inputs [P,l] POINT then LINE, args {}, outputType POINT; the mirror image of P in l, P not on l), PERPENDICULAR_THROUGH(inputs [P,l] POINT then LINE, args {}, outputType LINE; the line through P perpendicular to l), TRANSLATE_POINT(inputs [P], args {dx,dy} typedScalar, outputType POINT) and TRANSLATE_LINE(inputs [l], args {dx,dy} typedScalar, outputType LINE), the image shifted by dx along x and dy along y. dx and dy are literal numbers from the stored solution, not both 0; a translation given by letters, by a vector between two points, a ratio with a letter, or a negative ratio is unsupported: answer UNSUPPORTED. The image of a point under these operations is a derived point and must never carry raw coordinates; name it with a single capital letter, and when the solution writes the image with a prime (A with a prime mark) use a plain id such as P and say so in a source condition. PLANE MARKS 2 (E7, E8b, E12b): planeMarks.dimensions:[{id,from,to,label,unit?,labelKind?,side,style?,offset?,value?}] writes a length between two displayed points on a line parallel to them, outside the figure: side is the screen side ABOVE|BELOW|LEFT|RIGHT (the perpendicular of the two points that points that way; a segment nearly perpendicular to the named side is DIMENSION_SIDE_AMBIGUOUS), style BAR (default: thin extension lines, a dimension line, a short tick at each end), ARROW (arrowheads) or BRACE (a curly brace pointing outward), offset 1..3 stacks several on one side (24 px from the points, 44 px more per level), the label stands outside the line, centred; label is math (a trailing "\\,cm" is the unit cm), unit cm|mm|m, labelKind TEXT for plain text, value (optional) is the real distance (DIMENSION_FACT_MISMATCH); codes DIMENSION_SIDE_INVALID, DIMENSION_STYLE_INVALID, DIMENSION_OFFSET_INVALID, DIMENSION_LABEL_REQUIRED, DIMENSION_POINTS_COINCIDE, DIMENSION_TOO_SHORT; the observer recomputes the feet of the perpendiculars, offset, side, ticks, arrowheads, brace and label place (DIMENSION_OFFSET_MISMATCH, DIMENSION_SIDE_MISMATCH, DIMENSION_END_MISMATCH, DIMENSION_EXTENSION_MISMATCH, DIMENSION_TICK_MISMATCH, DIMENSION_ARROW_MISMATCH, DIMENSION_BRACE_MISMATCH, DIMENSION_PART_MISSING, DIMENSION_LABEL_NOT_OUTSIDE, DIMENSION_LABEL_OFF_CENTER, DIMENSION_LABEL_TOO_FAR, DIMENSION_LABEL_AMBIGUOUS_OWNER, DIMENSION_CROSSES_STROKE). planeMarks.pathLabels:[{id,points,label,unit?,labelKind?,value?}] writes ONE total length for a bent path: points are 3..8 distinct displayed points joined by drawn displaySegments, the label stands beside the path on a leader that starts on it (PATH_LABEL_POINTS_INVALID, PATH_LABEL_SEGMENT_NOT_DRAWN, PATH_LABEL_FACT_MISMATCH, PATH_LABEL_LABEL_REQUIRED; observer PATH_LABEL_LEADER_MISSING, PATH_LABEL_LEADER_CROSSES). angles[].labelPlacement "LEADER" puts that angle label outside its angle on a leader from its own arc even where it would fit inside (ANGLE_LABEL_PLACEMENT_INVALID, ANGLE_LABEL_PLACEMENT_NEEDS_LABEL; observer ANGLE_LABEL_LEADER_MISSING, ANGLE_LABEL_NOT_OUTSIDE_ANGLE). traces[].span "JOIN" draws one continuous arc through all stated intersections (spanDeg/2 past each end) instead of a short arc around each (TRACE_SPAN_INVALID, TRACE_JOIN_TOO_WIDE); traces[].labelAt START|MID|END (the counter-clockwise start, middle, end of the drawn arc) or a direction in degrees (0 right, 90 up) puts the label there (TRACE_LABEL_AT_INVALID, TRACE_LABEL_AT_NEEDS_LABEL, TRACE_LABEL_NOT_AT_ANCHOR); expressionLabels[].labelAt START|MID|END writes a segment label at its first end, middle or second end (SEGMENT_LABEL_AT_INVALID, SEGMENT_LABEL_NOT_AT_END). A composite panel caption of plain text wider than its cell now breaks into two lines and a caption taller than the standard row makes the caption rows taller; COMPOSITE_CAPTION_TOO_LARGE remains when it still does not fit. PLANE VECTORS (G3): a label text may name a vector as vec(a) (one lower-case letter) or vec(AB) (two different capital point names of the figure), alone or inside an expression such as vec(a)+vec(b), 2vec(a)-vec(b) or (-3vec(a)+vec(b))/4; it is typeset as an arrow over the letter(s); an empty, longer, nested or numeric name is VECTOR_LABEL_INVALID, a point the figure lacks is VECTOR_LABEL_UNKNOWN_POINT, and a backslash or dollar sign stays refused. planeMarks.vectors:[{id,from,to,label?,labelAt?,side?,ownership?,emphasis?,dashed?,components?,componentLabels?}] draws an arrow from point from to point to (id is the drawn segment id; never also list that segment in displaySegments), labelled by label; a point that ends an arrow and starts none is drawn without its dot (its name stays); components:true on a coordinate figure with axes draws dashed perpendiculars from the tip to both axes (feet without dots, numbered by componentLabels:[x,y]). Errors: VECTOR_ENDPOINT_INVALID, VECTOR_DUPLICATE, VECTOR_COMPONENTS_REQUIRE_AXES, VECTOR_COMPONENT_DEGENERATE, CALLOUT_VECTOR_UNSUPPORTED; observer codes VECTOR_DIRECTION_MISMATCH, VECTOR_TIP_DOT_PRESENT, VECTOR_COMPONENT_MISMATCH, VECTOR_LABEL_TEX_MISMATCH. planeMarks.vectorOps:[{id,op:SUM|DIFF|SCALE,of:[vector ids],point?,label?,labelAt?,side?,guides?,k?,emphasis?}] builds on vectors: SUM of two vectors with one start A (A to B, A to C) is the arrow A to D with D=B+C-A (point names a new point the engine computes exactly, or a point the figure already has, which must be exactly there: VECTOR_SUM_POINT_MISMATCH; guides PARALLELOGRAM adds the dashed sides B-D and C-D, TRIANGLE the dashed arrow B to D); DIFF is the arrow C to B; SCALE with the rational k (a string such as 2 or -1/2, never 0 or 1) is the arrow A to P with P=A+k(B-A). Exact operations VECTOR_SUM_POINT (inputs A,B,C) and VECTOR_SCALE_POINT (inputs A,B; args {k}) back them. Errors: VECTOR_OP_REQUIRES_COMMON_START, VECTOR_OP_UNKNOWN_VECTOR, VECTOR_SUM_DEGENERATE, VECTOR_SCALE_INVALID; observer codes VECTOR_SUM_POINT_MISMATCH, VECTOR_SUM_GUIDE_NOT_PARALLEL, VECTOR_DIFF_MISMATCH, VECTOR_SCALE_MISMATCH. A vector label is written as an expression only (vec(AB)+vec(AF)), never as an equation that states the answer. For source-coordinate geometry only, coordinateCanvas:{width,height} may enlarge the composition with width 384–720 and height 320–600; this changes framing only and must preserve all source coordinates, exact derived coordinates, labels and their owners. For a quadratic overview only, overviewCanvasHeight may increase the plot height between 400 and 600 SVG units; keep all required roots, vertex, source ticks and both curve arms visible. MULTI-PANEL PLANE FIGURES (composite-panels-v1): to compare several axis-free figures side by side (five option quadrilaterals, or two sub-items of one question) use {capability:"composite-panels-v1",panels:[{caption:{math?|text?},plan:PLANE_FIGURE_v1 plan},...],caption,sourceConditions,newVisualInformation}. Every panel must be a construction-spike-v1 PLANE_FIGURE_v1 plan (2 to 5 panels); a graph or number-line panel cannot be mixed in. A panel may instead be a solid-figure-v1 plan (solids or net, a prism, pyramid, net and so on, without caption, solidCaption, sourceConditions, newVisualInformation or sourceQuote), alone or beside plane figures: a grid with a solid panel has 2 to 6 panels (two columns, up to three rows: COMPOSITE_TOO_TALL when the page height is exceeded), the same solid may appear in each with a different group of segments emphasized, and the engine narrows each solid panel to 384 units so the two-column grid stays 776 units wide (a solid panel is refused with PANEL_SOLID_PLAN_<index>, a solid mixed with a graph or number line with PANEL_SOLID_MIXED_<index>, a bad panel count with PANELS). The engine builds a two-column grid (2 panels in one row, 3 or 5 panels in two columns over two or three rows, 4 a 2x2 grid), centres each audited panel SVG in an equal cell and writes one caption row per panel; it never uses three columns, because one 384-unit panel is the narrowest canvas and a 3*384 px row pushes the measured base font past the 32 px policy cap in the about 298 CSS px Archive solution column (UNSUPPORTED_DISPLAY_ENVELOPE:REQUESTED_PROFILE_FONT_SCALE). The two-column grid must keep the same envelope: the combined width should stay at or below about 848 SVG units for that measured column, so 384-420 wide panels pass while two 640-wide panels (1288) are refused at measure; narrow the canvas to 384-416 or use separate single plans when a wide figure is required. Each panel keeps the planeMarks/rightAngles/regions/displaySegments rules above with at least one SOURCE_POINT, no NORMALIZATION node and coordinateCanvas 384-720 by 320-600 (default 384x320); every point a mark or segment names must be in that panel displayPoints. Panel caption text is at most 24 characters; put the whole explanation in the top-level caption. A composite taller than the 640 CSS px page limit is refused (COMPOSITE_TOO_TALL), as are mixed panels (PANEL_PLANE_MIXED:<index>), 1 or 6+ panels, a wrong panel schema/canvas, and a caption wider than its cell (COMPOSITE_CAPTION_TOO_LARGE). COLOUR GROUPS (C1): the same thing wears the same colour, from the palette BLUE WARM GREEN ROSE VIOLET (BLUE and WARM first; at most 2-3 groups per figure, and a group also keeps its relation mark such as ticks or arc counts). planeMarks.regions[].fill takes the five names besides LIGHT and MEDIUM; planeMarks.equalAreas:[[region ids]] names regions of equal area (recomputed, relative error 1e-6) that share one palette colour no other region uses; planeMarks.angles[].color or planeMarks.equalAngles:[{angles:[angle ids],color}] colours angles of one size (0.5 degrees) at once; planeMarks.segmentColors:[{segments:[displaySegments ids],color}] draws corresponding or related segments in the palette line colour; equalLengths[].color and parallels[].color colour a group segments and marks. Errors: REGION_EQUAL_AREA_MISMATCH, REGION_EQUAL_AREA_COLOR_MISMATCH, REGION_COLOR_GROUP_AMBIGUOUS, ANGLE_COLOR_INVALID, ANGLE_COLOR_GROUP_MISMATCH, SEGMENT_COLOR_INVALID, SEGMENT_COLOR_UNKNOWN_SEGMENT, SEGMENT_COLOR_DUPLICATE, SEGMENT_COLOR_GROUP_MISMATCH. IF AND ONLY IF the source visual is a rational graph of capability 'rational-spike-v1', apply this separate bounded contract. graphPlan.family must be 'rational', numerator and denominator must each be exactly two integer/rational coefficient strings in ascending powers, the denominator's x coefficient must be nonzero, the numerator's x coefficient is nonzero (linear over linear) or zero with a nonzero constant (inverse proportion y=k/(d0+d1*x), described below), and sourceDomain must be exactly {kind:'ALL_REALS'} (meaning no additional interval restriction; natural denominator exclusions remain). The denominator therefore has exactly one simple real root. The numerator either does not vanish there (one vertical pole) or vanishes there (one removable hole); the overview must mark that pole or open hole and the exact horizontal asymptote y=numeratorLeading/denominatorLeading. Preserve the original denominator exclusion even when it cancels. The observer independently recomputes the singularity kind/location, horizontal asymptote, numerator zero, and visible branch coverage from exact coefficients, then checks pole-side separation, asymptote markers, and hole marker on the final SVG. Numerator/denominator degree above one, multiple denominator roots, nonreal denominator roots, oblique asymptotes, additional source intervals, and sub-resolution features are unsupported. INVERSE PROPORTION (nonzero constant numerator, middle school y=k/x or y=k/(d0+d1*x)): one vertical pole at x=-d0/d1 and the horizontal asymptote y=0, which is the x-axis; there is no x-intercept and no hole. An asymptote that coincides with a coordinate axis is the axis itself, so no dashed line is drawn over it (y=0 always; x=0 for y=k/x), and only a vertical asymptote off the y-axis is dashed and labelled x=p. The figure keeps one scale on both axes (a vertical squeeze of at most 1/2 only after a failure) and shows both branches. Optional graphPlan.formulaLabel (at most 40 characters of formula text such as y=b/x) replaces the printed formula for a symbolic constant; requiredPoints {id,x,y} are drawn as dots with their coordinates (exactCoordinates gives exact text). A zero numerator is refused with UNSUPPORTED_RATIONAL_NUMERATOR_ZERO and a pole too far from the origin to share one scale with it with EQUAL_UNIT_CURVE_TOO_NARROW. The observer recomputes the pole, the branch sides, which asymptotes are drawn (none on an axis) and every required point from the exact coefficients and reads the final SVG. No polynomial quadratic/cubic framing clause applies to this rational family. IF AND ONLY IF the source graph is the square root of an affine radicand, use the distinct capability 'sqrt-affine-spike-v1' and graphPlan.family 'sqrt-affine'. radicand must be exactly [constant,x coefficient] as exact integer/rational or p+q*sqrt(r) strings with a nonzero x coefficient. The general form y=q+s*k*sqrt(a*(x-p)) uses the same plan with optional exact amplitude k>0 (default 1), sign s in {1,-1} (default 1) and offset q (default 0); the starting point is (p,q) with p=-constant/a, a>0 opens the branch to the right (x>=p) and a<0 to the left (x<=p), and s*k>0 runs the tail upward while s*k<0 runs it downward. sourceDomain must be exactly {kind:'NATURAL_SQRT_AFFINE'} for the full natural domain on the branch side, or {kind:'CLOSED_INTERVAL',range:[exact lower,exact upper]} for a closed finite source interval inside the natural domain. For the natural mode the sampled domain starts exactly at that root; for CLOSED_INTERVAL the sampled domain must equal the source interval byte-for-byte in numeric value and must never be expanded. Open intervals, intervals outside the natural domain, a zero radicand slope, nonlinear or mixed radicands, and unreviewed compositions are unsupported. Derive and mark every closed source endpoint (both ends for CLOSED_INTERVAL). requiredPoints are {id,x,y} points on the curve; exactCoordinates {id:[exact x,exact y]} prints their exact coordinate labels; domainRangeLabels:true writes the domain and range inequalities. Independently verify the final curve begins/ends at the bound endpoint(s), contains no points outside the source domain, and moves in the single monotone direction implied by a*s*k. The viewport may have a display-only margin beyond the drawn domain, but that margin is not part of the sampled domain. IF AND ONLY IF the source graph is an absolute value of an affine function with an outer affine scale/offset, use the distinct capability 'absolute-value-spike-v1' and graphPlan.family 'absolute-value'. coefficients must be exactly [b,a,k,c] as rational strings for y=k*abs(a*x+b)+c; a and k must both be nonzero and sourceDomain must be exactly {kind:'ALL_REALS'}. Restricted source intervals, multiple absolute-value operators, mixed compositions, and a zero inner slope/outer scale are unsupported. Derive the exact corner, each real x-intercept when present, left/right branch slopes and tail directions. The final curve must keep two visible affine arms meeting at the exact corner; mark the corner with one closed outlined marker. The independent observer checks both one-sided slopes and actual-profile arm span/rise; do not smooth or bridge the corner with one chord. IF AND ONLY IF the source graph is exactly one two-branch piecewise-affine function on a finite closed interval, use the separate capability 'piecewise-affine-spike-v1' and graphPlan.family 'piecewise-affine'. sourceDomain must be exactly {kind:'CLOSED_INTERVAL',range:[lo,hi]} with canonical integer/rational strings and lo<hi. Include exactly one strict interior breakpoint and exactly two affine branches with rational coefficient strings; no nested cases, overlapping breakpoints, open outer domain, or unreviewed extra domains are supported. Set graphPlan.domain to the numeric values of the exact source interval without expansion. The piecewise field must declare {breakX,owner,left,right}; owner is LEFT or RIGHT and determines the function value at the breakpoint. Derive both one-sided limits exactly: if they differ, render a closed marker at the owner value and an open marker at the non-owner limit, with separate curves and no connecting segment; if they are equal, render one closed marker. Both outer source endpoints are closed and marked. The independent observer must recompute interval ownership and branch equations from exact coefficients, then verify each final polyline covers only its own interval, reaches its exact endpoint, and has no cross-break chord; overlapping or ambiguous ownership is unsupported. IF AND ONLY IF the source graph is exactly y=A*exp(k*x)+C with natural exponential (or the same shape with an explicit positive base and optional exponent shift, below), use the separate capability 'exponential-affine-spike-v1' and graphPlan.family 'exponential-affine'. coefficients must be exact rational strings [A,k,C], A and k nonzero, and sourceDomain exactly {kind:'ALL_REALS'}. The frozen overview binds the exact reference point at exponent zero, strict monotonicity sign(A*k), horizontal asymptote y=C and the side/approach direction, and growth-side direction. Keep x=0 visible, sample only a display overview that retains the original all-real source-domain meaning, and show the asymptote plus reference marker. Mixed exponents, non-natural bases, compositions, restricted source domains, and unsupported scale ranges are unsupported. The independent observer recomputes the function, monotonicity, reference and asymptote from exact coefficients, then checks the final curve and marker at the measured profile. BASE SUPPORT (exponential-affine-spike-v1): add graphPlan.base as an exact positive integer string ('2', '3'), fraction string ('1/2', '3/2') or 'sqrt(n)' (n a square-free integer > 1) and optionally graphPlan.shiftX (exact integer/fraction string, the horizontal shift p) for y=A*base^(k*(x-p))+C. The reference point is (p,A+C), the asymptote y=C, and for base<1 the growth side flips (k>0 with base<1 grows to the left). Natural base e is the omitted default. The formula label is typeset from the coefficients and base. A symbolic base, base<=0 or base=1, nested/unsimplified radicals, decimals and unsupported scale ranges are refused with an explicit base error code. TWO CURVES / INVERSE PAIR: use capability 'exp-log-pair-spike-v1' with graphPlan {family:'exp-log-pair',curves:[{id,family:'exponential-affine'|'logarithmic-affine',base,coefficients,sourceDomain,shiftX?},...],identityLine:true|false} (exactly two curves, at most one of each family is typical); the engine frames both curves and draws their asymptotes/boundaries, reference markers and the dashed identity line y=x. The observer recomputes each curve, its reference point and asymptote/boundary independently. IF AND ONLY IF the source graph is exactly y=A*ln(k*x+b)+C with natural logarithm, use the separate capability 'logarithmic-affine-spike-v1' and graphPlan.family 'logarithmic-affine'. coefficients must be exact rational strings [A,k,b,C], A and k nonzero, and sourceDomain exactly {kind:'NATURAL_LOG_AFFINE'} meaning k*x+b>0. The exact open boundary is x=-b/k; the reference point uses argument 1 at x=(1-b)/k and y=C. The overview samples only inside the natural domain and shows the boundary with a dashed vertical cue, the reference marker, strict monotonicity sign(A*k), and the correct one-sided unbounded trend. Restricted intervals and mixed compositions are unsupported. The independent observer recomputes boundary/reference/monotonicity from exact coefficients and checks the final curve stays on the valid side and approaches the boundary correctly at measured size. LOG BASE SUPPORT (logarithmic-affine-spike-v1): add graphPlan.base exactly as for the exponential family (integer, fraction or sqrt(n); natural e is the omitted default) for y=A*log_base(k*x+b)+C. The open boundary x=-b/k and the reference point argument 1 at x=(1-b)/k are unchanged; a base<1 reverses the increasing/decreasing direction. A symbolic base, base<=0 or base=1, nested radicals and decimals are refused with an explicit base error code. IF AND ONLY IF the source graph is one uncomposed sine, cosine, or tangent function y=A*F(k*x+phasePi*pi)+C, use the distinct capability 'trigonometric-spike-v1' and graphPlan.family 'trigonometric'. F is exactly SIN, COS, or TAN; coefficients are exact rational strings [A,k,C], A and k are nonzero, and phasePi is a canonical rational multiple of pi. SIN/COS require sourceDomain {kind:'ALL_REALS'} and the overview displays one full exact period, marking and independently checking both extrema and all midline crossings (including roots where y=0). TAN requires sourceDomain {kind:'ALL_REALS_WITH_TAN_POLES'} and displays exactly one complete branch between adjacent poles with both dashed pole cues, no cross-pole chord, and monotonic direction sign(A*k). Mixed identities, powers/compositions, restricted source intervals, and unsupported non-natural phase representations are unsupported. The independent observer recomputes period, phase, amplitude/midline, feature locations or poles and branch coverage from exact coefficients and verifies the final rendered SVG at measured profiles. SEVERAL PERIODS WITH REFERENCE LINES (SIN or COS only): for a solution that counts the crossings of y=A*F(k*x+phasePi*pi)+C with a horizontal or slanted line on an explicit interval, set graphPlan.sourceDomain:{kind:'INTERVAL_PI',rangePi:[lo,hi],states:[start,end]} (lo<hi exact canonical rational multiples of pi such as \"0\" and \"6\"; each state OPEN or CLOSED, CLOSED when omitted; from 1/4 to 12 periods) and graphPlan.referenceLines:[{id,y,intersectionCount}] for a horizontal line or [{id,slope,intercept,intersectionCount}] for a slanted one (at most three; id one letter then letters or digits; y, slope, intercept exact integer or fraction strings, slope not 0; a letter parameter is drawn at one sample value that the caption states). intersectionCount is the author's claim of how many times the line meets the curve inside the interval (a touching point counts once, an open end is not in the interval). The engine finds every crossing (closed form for a horizontal line, sign changes for a slanted one) and the independent observer finds them again with SymPy and 40-digit root scans of the gap and its derivative, then checks the final SVG: the curve over exactly the interval, both end markers (hollow for OPEN, filled for CLOSED, with the end label k*pi), each line and one dot per crossing. domain and viewport are reframed by the engine. Refused with a code: TRIG_LINE_INTERSECTION_COUNT_MISMATCH:<id>:engine finds N intersection(s) (wrong claim, or a slanted line that only touches the curve), TRIG_LINE_THROUGH_INTERVAL_END:<id> (a crossing at a closed end), TRIG_INTERVAL_PERIOD_COUNT_UNSUPPORTED, INVALID_TRIG_INTERVAL, TRIG_LINE_FORM_INVALID:<id>, TRIG_LINE_SLOPE_ZERO:<id>, TRIG_LINE_COEFFICIENT_INVALID:<id>, TRIG_LINE_INTERSECTION_COUNT_INVALID:<id>, TRIG_REFERENCE_LINES_INVALID, TRIG_REFERENCE_LINES_REQUIRE_INTERVAL_PI (lines on the one-period form), TRIGONOMETRIC_SOURCE_DOMAIN_UNSUPPORTED (TAN or a malformed interval). GENERAL PARAMETERS (ratePi/phase) EXTEND THIS. graphPlan.ratePi carries the pi factor in the rate: when it is present the rate is ratePi*pi and coefficients are [A,C] (A amplitude, C midline); when it is absent the rate is coefficients[1] (plain, no pi) with coefficients [A,k,C]. The phase is exactly one of phasePi (a rational multiple of pi) or phase (radians). The period is 2/|rate| for SIN/COS and 1/|rate| for TAN. On an interval the sourceDomain is either {kind:'INTERVAL',range:[lo,hi],states:[start,end]} measured in plain x units, which requires ratePi, or {kind:'INTERVAL_PI',rangePi:[lo,hi],states:[start,end]} measured in pi units, which requires a plain rate and a pi-multiple phase; from 1/4 to 12 periods. keyPoints:true (SIN/COS with a pi-multiple phase only) marks every exact extremum and midline crossing inside the interval, at most 60. axisTickPi:true on a pi-unit interval relabels axisTickValues.x as exact nonzero multiples of pi (pi/2, pi, 3*pi/2) that must lie inside the view. TAN on an interval draws every branch between its poles with each pole dashed, requires at least one pole strictly inside, and refuses a pole at a closed end or any referenceLines. Refused with a code: TRIG_GRAMMAR_UNSUPPORTED, INVALID_TRIGONOMETRIC_RATE, TRIGONOMETRIC_COEFFICIENT_INVALID, INVALID_TRIGONOMETRIC_PHASE, TRIGONOMETRIC_NONZERO_SCALE_AND_RATE_REQUIRED, TRIG_INTERVAL_REQUIRES_RATE_PI, TRIG_RATE_PI_REQUIRES_PLAIN_INTERVAL, TRIG_INTERVAL_PI_PHASE_MUST_BE_PI, TRIG_PHASE_RATIONAL_REQUIRES_INTERVAL, TRIG_KEY_POINTS_REQUIRE_SIN_COS, TRIG_KEY_POINTS_REQUIRE_RATIONAL_PHASE, TRIG_KEY_POINTS_TOO_MANY, INVALID_TRIG_KEY_POINTS, TRIG_TAN_REFERENCE_LINES_UNSUPPORTED, TRIG_TAN_REQUIRES_PI_PHASE, TRIG_TAN_POLE_AT_INTERVAL_END, TRIG_TAN_INTERVAL_NO_POLE, TRIG_TICK_PI_REQUIRES_PI_AXIS, INVALID_TRIG_TICK_PI_FLAG, INVALID_TRIG_AXIS_TICK_VALUES, TRIG_PI_TICK_VALUE_INVALID, TRIG_PI_TICK_OUTSIDE_VIEW, UNSUPPORTED_TRIGONOMETRIC_NUMERIC_SCOPE. The independent observer recomputes the curve samples, key points, crossings, poles and pi tick labels from the same exact parameters and checks the final SVG. A function graph made of up to four polynomial pieces of degree at most 2 that join without a jump (for example the absolute value of a quadratic written as its pieces, or |x^2+x-6|+2x when the solution rewrites the equation as k=g(x)), drawn together with up to three horizontal lines y=c and their intersection points, is ONE curve and is supported: use {capability:"piecewise-polynomial-spike-v1",graphPlan:{family:"piecewise-polynomial",pieces:[{coefficients:[...]},...],breakpoints:[{x,y},...],sourceDomain,formula,referenceLines:[...],requiredPoints:[...],exactCoordinates:{...},axisTickValues:{x:[],y:[]}},caption,sourceConditions,newVisualInformation}; every field named here sits inside graphPlan except capability, caption, sourceConditions and newVisualInformation (and the optional top-level coordinatePlacement, coordinatePanel and labelDirections, which work as for a quadratic). pieces lists 1 to 4 pieces from left to right, each {coefficients:[constant, x coefficient, x squared coefficient]} with exact integer or fraction strings such as "-6", "3", "1/2" (degree at most 2; no decimals; an irrational coefficient is allowed only as described under IRRATIONAL COEFFICIENTS below). breakpoints has exactly one entry fewer than pieces: breakpoints[i].x (exact strings, strictly increasing) is where piece i ends and piece i+1 begins, and breakpoints[i].y is the common value there; both neighbouring pieces must give exactly that y (a jump is refused with PIECEWISE_POLY_DISCONTINUOUS, a wrong y with PIECEWISE_POLY_BREAKPOINT_VALUE_MISMATCH). The first piece runs from the left end of the source domain to the first breakpoint, the last piece from the last breakpoint to the right end. sourceDomain is {kind:"ALL_REALS"} (the outer pieces continue without end; the engine draws them a little beyond the outermost marked point) or {kind:"INTERVAL",range:[lo,hi],states:[start,end]} as for a quadratic on an interval (each breakpoint strictly inside). requiredPoints:[{id,x,y}] with numeric x and y must contain every breakpoint (corner) and the vertex of every quadratic piece whose vertex lies strictly inside that piece; the engine recomputes both and refuses a plan that misses one (PIECEWISE_POLY_CORNER_NOT_IN_REQUIRED_POINTS, PIECEWISE_POLY_EXTREMUM_NOT_IN_REQUIRED_POINTS) or a point off the curve; give non-integer coordinates in exactCoordinates {pointId:["1/2","25/4"]} so every such point gets its exact coordinate label, and use one capital letter as id (A, B, C) so a crowded label can move to the band. formula (optional) writes the header line y=... : the source function in x with digits, x, abs(...), + - * / ^ and parentheses only, implicit products such as 2x allowed (for example "abs(x^2+x-6)+2x"); it must be the same function as the pieces (PIECEWISE_POLY_FORMULA_MISMATCH otherwise); leave it out when the source gives the function only by its pieces. referenceLines:[{id,y,intersectionX,labelIntersections}] (at most 3): id is a short ASCII name starting with a letter (for example k), y the exact height as an integer or fraction string, and intersectionX the exact x of EVERY intersection of that line with the whole curve, each once (a touching point counts once), written with integers, fractions, sqrt(...), + - * / and parentheses (for example "(-3-sqrt(53))/2"); the engine solves every piece exactly and refuses a list that differs (PIECEWISE_POLY_INTERSECTION_MISMATCH says how many it finds). Each intersection is drawn as a dot; labelIntersections:true also writes its exact coordinates beside the dot (default false: dots only, which keeps a crowded figure readable). Each line is written as y=c in the band under the formula. When the solution counts intersections with a moving line y=k, draw one representative value strictly inside the answer range (for example y=5 for 4<k<25/4) and name that value in the caption. Do not give domain or viewport: the engine frames the figure at one scale on both axes. A referenceLines entry may instead be a SLANTED line y=m*x+c: {id,slope,intercept,intersectionX} with slope (not 0) and intercept exact integer, fraction or p+q*sqrt(r) strings; it is drawn across the figure with a dot at every intersection, its equation goes to the band, and intersectionX again lists EVERY intersection x with the whole curve (PIECEWISE_POLY_INTERSECTION_MISMATCH otherwise). A moving line (y=mx+12 with m asked for, y=x+k, y=p(x-3)+3) is drawn at ONE sample value of the parameter strictly inside the answer range or at the boundary case the solution discusses; name that value in the caption. labelIntersections is not available for a slanted line (PIECEWISE_POLY_SLANTED_LINE_LABELS_UNSUPPORTED): to write the coordinates of an intersection with rational coordinates, list it in requiredPoints (id, x, y, exactCoordinates) and it is drawn once as that key point. Slope 0 with slope/intercept is refused (PIECEWISE_POLY_SLANTED_LINE_SLOPE_ZERO: use y); a slope or intercept that is not an exact integer, fraction or p+q*sqrt(r) string (a decimal, a letter) is PIECEWISE_POLY_SLANTED_LINE_COEFFICIENT_INVALID; a line equal to a whole piece is PIECEWISE_POLY_REFERENCE_LINE_CONTAINS_A_PIECE. A vertical line, a jump, or a piece of degree 3 or more is not supported; a second function that is itself piecewise is written in referenceCurves (see REFERENCE CURVES below). A discontinuous function made of up to six polynomial pieces of degree at most 2, each on its own bounded or unbounded interval with OPEN or CLOSED ends, is supported: use {capability:"piecewise-discontinuous-spike-v1",graphPlan:{family:"piecewise-discontinuous",pieces:[{coefficients:[...],domain:[lo,hi],start:"OPEN"|"CLOSED",end:"OPEN"|"CLOSED"},...],jumpValues:[{x,y},...],referenceLines:[...],requiredPoints:[...],exactCoordinates:{...},axisTickValues:{x:[],y:[]}},caption,sourceConditions,newVisualInformation}. coefficients are the constant, x and x squared terms as exact integer, fraction or p+q*sqrt(r) strings (degree at most 2); domain holds the two ends of that piece, a number or exact string, or null for an unbounded end (only the first left end and the last right end may be null). start and end default CLOSED and say whether the piece includes that end; the piece is drawn only on its own interval, so pieces at a jump are never joined by a connecting segment. Pieces are listed left to right and must not overlap (PIECEWISE_DOMAIN_OVERLAP); more than six is PIECEWISE_TOO_MANY_PIECES; a bad coefficient or domain is PIECEWISE_DISCONTINUOUS_PIECE_INVALID and a bad state is PIECEWISE_ENDPOINT_STATE_INVALID. At a junction where the two pieces share an x, exactly one function value must be declared: either one of the two ends is CLOSED (a filled dot at that value) or jumpValues has {x,y} there; none gives PIECEWISE_DISCONTINUITY_UNDECLARED and a declared value that differs from the CLOSED end gives PIECEWISE_VALUE_CONFLICT. jumpValues also writes an isolated point f(a)=c where no piece is defined, and the engine draws a filled dot there; the value dots and the hollow dots at OPEN ends are computed and drawn by the engine, never listed by the author. A staircase is written instead as step:{values:[...],xFrom,xTo}: values[i] on the i-th equal subinterval (closed on the left, open on the right, the last closed at xTo), up to six steps, a bad interval is STEP_INTERVAL_INVALID (do not give pieces and step together). referenceLines (at most 3) are horizontal y=c exactly as for piecewise-polynomial-spike-v1: {id,y,intersectionX,labelIntersections}, and intersectionX lists EVERY crossing with the whole function including a declared value that lies on the line; an OPEN end is not a crossing and a piece equal to the line is PIECEWISE_REFERENCE_LINE_CONTAINS_A_PIECE; a differing list is PIECEWISE_INTERSECTION_MISMATCH. requiredPoints must contain the vertex of every quadratic piece that lies strictly inside its piece (PIECEWISE_DISCONTINUOUS_EXTREMUM_NOT_IN_REQUIRED_POINTS); a key point lies on a piece or on a declared value. Do not give formula or referenceCurves: a formula for a genuinely discontinuous function and a second piecewise curve are not supported. Do not give domain or viewport: the engine frames the figure at one scale on both axes. IRRATIONAL COEFFICIENTS (piecewise-polynomial-spike-v1): when the figure needs a parabola or a line whose coefficient is irrational, for example a tangent condition k=-3+2*sqrt(3) in y=x^2+kx+5, a line of slope 6-2*sqrt(7), the line y=-11+4*sqrt(7), or a quadratic with a=2-sqrt(2) on [0,2], write that coefficient as an exact string p+q*sqrt(r) with p and q integers or fractions and r a positive integer: "-3+2*sqrt(3)", "3/2-sqrt(3)", "(1+sqrt(2))/2", "sqrt(7)/2". This works for pieces[].coefficients, breakpoints, referenceLines y, slope and intercept, and for the x and y of requiredPoints; use ONE piece (pieces has one entry, no breakpoints) for a plain quadratic. All irrational numbers of one plan use the same radical sqrt(r) (the engine computes in the field of p+q*sqrt(r)); a plan with sqrt(2) in one coefficient and sqrt(3) in another is PIECEWISE_POLY_IRRATIONAL_RADICANDS_MIXED. Nested radicals such as sqrt(2+sqrt(3)), two radicals in one coefficient (sqrt(2)+sqrt(3)) and cube roots are PIECEWISE_POLY_IRRATIONAL_FORM_UNSUPPORTED; a decimal such as 1.73 or a letter is PIECEWISE_POLY_COEFFICIENT_INVALID. A key point with irrational coordinates is given with exact strings, for example {id:"V",x:"3/2-sqrt(3)",y:"-1/4+3*sqrt(3)"}; the engine checks that it lies exactly on the curve (PIECEWISE_POLY_REQUIRED_POINT_NOT_ON_CURVE), writes it as the exact coordinate label and keeps the float for the drawing. The vertex of the parabola, every breakpoint and every contact point must still be listed in requiredPoints. intersectionX may contain nested radicals when the crossing really is one (for example "(10-2*sqrt(7)-sqrt(216-72*sqrt(7)))/4-2"); the engine and the independent observer both recompute it. The display formula cannot contain sqrt: a plan with an irrational piece and a formula is PIECEWISE_POLY_FORMULA_IRRATIONAL_UNSUPPORTED, leave formula out and name the case (for example k=-3+2*sqrt(3)) in the caption. Two cases that the solution compares (k=-3+2*sqrt(3) and k=-3-2*sqrt(3)) are two separate figures; a composite panel needs polynomial-spike-v1 and cannot hold irrational coefficients. When the stored solution solves a quadratic inequality (for example D/4=(a+3)(a-5)<0 for a parameter a, or x^2+4x+3<0 together with x>=-2), use {capability:"quadratic-inequality-chain-v1",inequality:{coefficients,comparator,variable,domain,adjoinedPoints,integerPoints},graphCaption,numberLineCaption,caption,sourceConditions,newVisualInformation}: the engine draws a fixed chain of two panels, the quadratic graph y=f(variable) with its real roots and vertex as key points, then the solution set as a number line headed by the interval (for example -3<=a<5 written with the inequality signs), with a filled dot at a closed end, a hollow dot at an open end and a thick band over the set. Give only the inequality f(variable) comparator 0: coefficients are exactly three exact integer or fraction strings [constant, linear, squared] of f with a nonzero squared coefficient (expand the factored form: (a+3)(a-5) is ["-15","-2","1"]); comparator is one of "<", "<=", ">", ">="; variable is the solution variable, one lowercase letter other than y (default x). Never give endpoints, dots or coordinates: the engine solves the inequality exactly (roots with square roots allowed) and an independent observer re-solves it and checks the drawing. domain (optional) {kind:"INTERVAL",range:[lo,hi],states:[start,end]} is a condition the solution imposes on the variable (for example a+3>0 or x>=-2): lo and hi are exact strings or null for no bound, each state OPEN or CLOSED; the set is intersected with it. adjoinedPoints (optional) lists exact values a separate case of the solution adds (for example ["-3"] when the solution checks a=-3 on its own and it satisfies the condition); each must close an open end of the set. integerPoints (optional) draws the integers of the set as dots above the line: true for a bounded set, or {range:["lo","hi"]} (integer strings) to count only integers in that range; use it when the solution counts or lists integers, natural numbers or their sum. graphCaption and numberLineCaption (optional) are panel captions {math,text} as for composite panels (math plain ASCII such as k>0; text short Korean without subscripts). The final set must be one interval, one point or all reals: a set of two pieces (INEQUALITY_SOLUTION_NOT_ONE_INTERVAL), an empty set (INEQUALITY_SOLUTION_EMPTY), an adjoined value that does not touch the set (INEQUALITY_ADJOINED_POINT_NOT_ADJACENT), integer points of an unbounded set without a range (INEQUALITY_INTEGER_POINTS_NEED_RANGE), more than 25 integer points, a linear inequality and a system of two quadratic inequalities are not supported; for a system, draw the one quadratic inequality and give the other condition as domain. When the stored solution compares solution sets on a number line (the intersection of two or more inequality solution sets, a solution set whose end depends on a parameter such as a or n, integer solutions, or an internal division point on the line) and no quadratic graph is needed, use {capability:"number-line-figure-v1",numberLine:{variable,parameter,rows},numberLineCaption,caption,sourceConditions,newVisualInformation}: the engine draws one panel with a row per set on one shared scale and never draws a coordinate plane. rows (1 to 4) is a list of {kind:"SET",pieces:[{lo,hi}],integerPoints}, {kind:"RESULT",integerPoints} or {kind:"POINTS",points}. A SET row is the solution set of one inequality as one or two disjoint pieces (for example x<-1 or x>2 is two pieces); a piece is {lo,hi} where each end is null for a ray or {value:"exact integer or fraction string",state:"OPEN"|"CLOSED"} (OPEN for < and >, CLOSED for <= and >=) or {param:{mul:"exact",add:"exact"},state} for an end equal to mul*p+add with p the parameter (a-2 is mul "1" add "-2"; (a+2)/3 is mul "1/3" add "2/3"); the engine writes the end label itself, stacked fractions included. parameter:{name:"a",value:"exact"} is required exactly when some end uses param: name is one lowercase letter other than the variable and y, and value is the sample value of the parameter the figure is drawn at (the figure then says it is drawn for that value; give a value that shows the case the solution discusses, for example a boundary value, and state it in caption). RESULT (at most one, after at least two SET rows, last) is the exact intersection of every SET row above it, computed and checked by the engine: never give its ends. integerPoints on a SET or RESULT row draws the integers of that row as dots over the line: true for a bounded set, or {range:["lo","hi"]} (integer strings) for a ray or to count only integers in a range; at most 25. A POINTS row (2 to 8 points, ids one capital letter or one Hangul consonant letter each) marks points by {id,value:"exact"} or an internal division point {id,divides:{from:"A",to:"B",ratio:["m","n"]}} which the engine places at AP:PB=m:n and labels with m over AP and n over PB. A point may carry name (null, or one Hangul consonant letter such as ㄱ), hideName:true (no name is drawn) and showValue:false (its value is not written; the point and its name are still drawn). A POINTS row may carry band:false (no highlighted segment between the outermost points) and subdivisions:[{from,to,parts}] (integer strings with from<to, parts 2 to 10, at most 8 entries that do not overlap): every integer from the lowest to the highest of the points and subdivisions then gets a labelled tick, each unit between from and to is cut into parts equal parts by short ticks, and the values of the points that show one are written above their names. numberLine.axisVariable:false leaves out the variable written at the end of each axis. variable (default x, one lowercase letter other than y) names the axis. numberLineCaption is a panel caption {math,text} as for composite panels. Refused with a code: a RESULT that is empty (NUMBER_LINE_RESULT_EMPTY), a single point (NUMBER_LINE_RESULT_IS_A_POINT) or more than two pieces (NUMBER_LINE_TOO_MANY_PIECES); pieces that overlap or touch (NUMBER_LINE_PIECES_OVERLAP, NUMBER_LINE_PIECES_TOUCH); a point piece or reversed ends (NUMBER_LINE_PIECE_IS_A_POINT, NUMBER_LINE_PIECE_INVALID); a param end without parameter (NUMBER_LINE_PARAMETER_MISSING), a parameter no end uses (NUMBER_LINE_PARAMETER_UNUSED), a bad name or sample value (NUMBER_LINE_PARAMETER_INVALID) or mul 0 (NUMBER_LINE_PARAMETER_COEFFICIENT_ZERO); decimals or non-exact values (NUMBER_LINE_VALUE_INVALID); integerPoints of an unbounded row without a range (NUMBER_LINE_INTEGER_POINTS_NEED_RANGE) or more than 25 integers (NUMBER_LINE_TOO_MANY_INTEGER_POINTS); coinciding points or a division that names a missing point (NUMBER_LINE_POINTS_COINCIDE, NUMBER_LINE_DIVISION_INVALID); a name that is not one Hangul consonant letter or contradicts hideName (NUMBER_LINE_POINT_NAME_INVALID), two points with one Hangul name in a row (NUMBER_LINE_POINT_NAME_DUPLICATE), subdivisions that are not integer units with 2 to 10 parts, overlap, or sit on a SET or RESULT row (NUMBER_LINE_SUBDIVISIONS_INVALID), ticks closer than 6 px (NUMBER_LINE_TICKS_TOO_DENSE) and a non-boolean axisVariable (NUMBER_LINE_AXIS_VARIABLE_INVALID). A solution set that depends on the parameter in a way that changes the shape of the figure from case to case is drawn for one sample value only; never present one case as the whole answer. An external division point, a set of three or more pieces and a shaded plane region are not supported. NUMBER LINE COMPASS ARCS AND LETTER POINTS (number-line-figure-v1): numberLine.constructions:[{id,center,radius:{value},arc:{side:"LEFT"|"RIGHT"|"BOTH"},hits:[{id,branch:"LEFT"|"RIGHT"}]}] (1 to 4; row:k only with two POINTS rows) draws a compass arc over the line round a drawn point of a POINTS row, with an exact radius (an exact string such as 3, 3/2, sqrt(5), 1+sqrt(5); one radical inside a value, but different points may use different radicals such as sqrt(2) and sqrt(3)), landing on the hit points; each hit is a point of the same row whose value must be exactly center+radius (branch RIGHT) or center-radius (LEFT); side RIGHT or LEFT lists one hit, BOTH lists two. An optional source:{grid:true,from:[x,0],to:[x,y],toName?} on a construction draws the segment whose length is the radius on a lattice of unit cells (one cell is one unit of the line) standing on the line: from is [the centre value (an integer), 0], to is a lattice point above and to one side of it (y from 1, x not the centre value), its length sqrt(dx^2+dy^2) must be the radius exactly, toName is an optional capital letter written at its end; the arc then runs from its hit up to that end. Chained constructions: source:{perpendicular:{at:"P",height:"1",toName?}} raises a perpendicular of an exact height on the line at the point at (usually the hit of an earlier arc) and draws the hypotenuse from the centre to its tip with a right angle mark; sqrt(|at-center|^2+height^2) must be the radius of that construction exactly (the spiral: radius sqrt(2) from the unit square diagonal, a perpendicular of height 1 at sqrt(2), the hypotenuse sqrt(3) is the next radius); the arc runs from its hit to the tip. A POINTS value may itself be such an exact surd string (for example "-5+2*sqrt(5)", whose label under the dot is written with a root sign), never a decimal. A POINTS point with neither value nor divides is a letter point: its id is one lowercase letter, optionally with a minus sign ("a", "-a", "n"), written under its dot in math italic and never with a number; its place comes only from constraints: between:[p,q] (ids of the row, or exact values such as "0" and "1"), mirrorOf:"a" (the reflection of the letter point a in 0; the two ids must be a and -a) and the row field order:[ids] left to right. The constraints must fix the order of every letter point (NUMBER_LINE_SYMBOL_ORDER_AMBIGUOUS otherwise); the engine then picks a place that satisfies them and writes none of it. A point style may also be "TICK" (a short vertical tick through the line, for an unknown such as n). Refused with a code: NUMBER_LINE_CONSTRUCTION_INVALID, _NEEDS_POINTS_ROW, _ROW_INVALID, _ROW_AMBIGUOUS, _POINT_UNKNOWN, _RADIUS_INVALID, _ARC_INVALID, _HIT_MISMATCH, _HITS_INCOMPLETE, _SPAN_ABOVE, _ARC_TOO_SMALL, _ARC_ON_LABEL, _ARC_ON_DOT, _SOURCE_INVALID, _SOURCE_LENGTH_MISMATCH, _SOURCE_TOO_TALL, _SOURCE_ON_LABEL; NUMBER_LINE_RADICALS_MIXED (inside one value, or a centre plus a radius with two radicals); NUMBER_LINE_SYMBOL_INVALID, _BETWEEN_INVALID, _ORDER_INVALID, _MIRROR_INVALID, _ORDER_AMBIGUOUS, _CONSTRAINT_CONTRADICTORY, _CONSTRAINT_UNSATISFIABLE, _UNSUPPORTED, _TOO_CLOSE. When the stored solution reads or explains a statistics or probability picture of the problem (a histogram, a frequency polygon, a relative-frequency graph, a stem-and-leaf plot, a box plot, a scatter plot, the 6x6 table of two dice, a probability tree), use {capability:"stat-chart-figure-v1",chart:{kind,...},caption,sourceConditions,newVisualInformation}: the engine draws the chart in textbook form with Korean axis titles and derives every other number itself. Give only the data of the problem, exactly. kind HISTOGRAM or FREQUENCY_POLYGON: boundaries (n+1 class boundaries as exact strings, equal class width, at least 0, at most 12 classes), series [{name?,frequencies:[one integer per class]}] or [{name?,relativeFrequencies:["0.1","3/20"]}] (relative frequencies must add up to 1; a polygon may have two named series, a histogram one), yKind "FREQUENCY" or "RELATIVE" (RELATIVE with frequencies makes the engine divide by the total), polygon true on a HISTOGRAM to draw its frequency polygon over it, xTitle and yTitle (short Korean, for example "기록(회)" and "도수(명)"), grid, showValues, highlightClasses; the engine adds the polygon end classes of frequency zero and the class midpoints itself. kind STEM_LEAF: values [whole numbers 0-999] (or groups [{name,values},{name,values}] for a back-to-back plot), unitText such as "건", title, key {stem,leaf}, marks [{group?,ranks:[rank in ascending order]} or {group?,median:true}] to circle the leaves; the engine sorts, keeps empty stems and counts. kind BOX_PLOT: series [{name,values:[at least 4 numbers]}] or [{name,summary:{min,q1,median,q3,max}}] (1 to 3 boxes), xTitle, step, showValues; quartiles follow the curriculum (Q1 and Q3 are the medians of the lower and upper half, the middle value left out when n is odd). kind SCATTER: points [{x,y,name?}] (every plotted point, at most 40), xRange and yRange [lo,hi] (at least 0, ends multiples of the step), xStep, yStep, labelEvery, xTitle, yTitle, grid, diagonal true for the line y=x. kind DICE_TABLE: faces (default 6), cell PAIR, SUM, PRODUCT or NONE, highlight as a condition on the two dice a (across) and b (down): {expr:"a+b",in:[2,7]}, {expr,cmp:"<=",value}, combined with any, all or not (expr uses integers, a, b, + - * and parentheses, for example "b-2*a"); the engine colours exactly the cells that satisfy it. kind TREE: root {label?,children:[{label,p:"3/7",children:[...]}]} with 2 to 5 children per node, depth at most 3, at most 12 leaves, exact probabilities p on every branch or on none (the probabilities of the children of a node must add up to 1), showPathProbability to write the product along each path at its leaf. kind DOT_PLOT (a dot plot): values [whole numbers 0-9999] (or counts [{value,count}]), step, axisBreak {from,to} for an outlier far from the rest, xTitle: one dot per datum stacked over its value. Refused with a code: unequal class widths (STAT_CLASS_WIDTH_UNEQUAL), a frequency list of the wrong length (STAT_FREQUENCY_COUNT_MISMATCH), a stated total that is not the sum (STAT_FREQUENCY_TOTAL_MISMATCH), relative frequencies not adding up to 1 (STAT_RELATIVE_SUM_NOT_ONE), a leaf mark outside the data (STAT_STEM_MARK_INVALID), fewer than 4 box-plot values (STAT_BOX_NEEDS_FOUR_VALUES), a point outside the axis range (STAT_POINT_OUTSIDE_RANGE), an expression that uses another letter (STAT_DICE_EXPR_INVALID), branch probabilities not adding up to 1 (STAT_TREE_BRANCH_PROBABILITY_SUM) or given on only some branches (STAT_TREE_PROBABILITY_MIXED), a title or label that cannot be placed (STAT_LABEL_OUT_OF_BOUNDS, STAT_LABELS_TOO_CLOSE). A pie chart, a category bar chart and a cumulative graph are not supported. kind NORMAL_CURVE (E36, inside stat-chart-figure-v1) draws a bell curve N(mean, sd^2) from the exact density: chart {kind:"NORMAL_CURVE",curves:[{id,mean,sd,label?,meanLine?}] (1 to 3, exact numbers, or letters "m","σ" for a problem that gives only letters: then one curve and z positions),ticks:["13",{x:"25",text:"k"},{z:"-1.5"}],shades:[{curve?,from:"13"|null,to:"20"|null,tone?}] (from and to name ticks by their text, null runs to the window end),areaLabels:[{shade,text:"0.4332"|"0.5+0.1915"|"p"|"?"}] (a number must equal the integrated area: use the problem table values, never the asked answer),symmetry:[{pair:["17","23"]}],zAxis,span}. SCATTER also takes refLines:[{type:"x=k"|"y=k",value}|{type:"y=x"}] and regions:[{xMin?,xMax?,yMin?,yMax?,markPoints?,countLabel?,countUnit?,tone?}] to mark the points of an area (countLabel only when the count is not the asked value). Refused with a code: STAT_NORMAL_SD_INVALID, STAT_NORMAL_AREA_LABEL_MISMATCH, STAT_NORMAL_BOUNDARY_NOT_TICKED, STAT_NORMAL_SYMMETRY_MISMATCH, STAT_NORMAL_WINDOW_TOO_WIDE, STAT_SCATTER_GUIDE_OUT_OF_RANGE, STAT_SCATTER_REGION_EMPTY. APPLICATION AXES (polynomial-spike-v1, a quadratic on sourceDomain INTERVAL only): for a word problem whose two axes measure different quantities (a count 0..50 against a profit 0..45000, a perimeter against an area) set graphPlan.applicationAxes:true. Each axis then has its own scale (the plot is 448 by 360 and the figure is never drawn at one scale), the author gives the real tick values as graphPlan.axisTickValues:{x:[...],y:[...]} (2 to 8 values per axis, an arithmetic progression with step 1, 2 or 5 times a power of ten, every value a multiple of the step, nonzero, inside the framed view; pick values that stay clear of the curve and the key points), and graphPlan.axisVariable may be one letter other than y, lower or upper case (n, P), for the horizontal axis. domain, viewport and requiredPoints work as for an interval quadratic (key points on the curve, endpoint dots at the interval ends); straight lines are allowed. A pure mathematical graph must never use it: it keeps one scale. The independent observer re-checks the plot size against the viewport and each axis scale and re-validates the ticks. Refused with a code: APPLICATION_AXES_REQUIRES_QUADRATIC_INTERVAL (any other family, ALL_REALS, cubic), INVALID_APPLICATION_AXES (the flag is not true, or axisSqueeze or plotCanvas is given), APPLICATION_AXES_TICKS_INVALID:x or :y (missing, empty, not 1-2-5 or outside the view), APPLICATION_AXES_CURVE_TOO_NARROW (the interval is under 48 px wide), APPLICATION_AXES_SCALE_MISMATCH (observer). REFERENCE CURVES (piecewise-polynomial-spike-v1): when the solution needs a second function drawn with the first (f and g crossing, the solution of f(x)<=g(x)), add graphPlan.referenceCurves (at most two). Each entry is EITHER a quadratic {id,name?,coefficients:[constant, x coefficient, x squared coefficient],intersectionX:[...]} with a nonzero x squared coefficient, OR a piecewise function {id,name?,pieces:[{coefficients},...] (1 to 4, degree at most 2),breakpoints:[{x,y},...] (one fewer than pieces; the pieces must meet there, a jump is PIECEWISE_POLY_REFERENCE_CURVE_DISCONTINUOUS),sourceDomain?,formula?,intersectionX:[...]}, where sourceDomain is omitted or {kind:"ALL_REALS"}, or {kind:"INTERVAL",range:[lo,hi],states:[start,end]} for its OWN domain (an OPEN end is drawn hollow and excluded as a crossing). id is a short ASCII name starting with a letter; name (optional) is one lowercase letter other than i, x and y and labels the curve in the band (name + "(x)=" + formula when formula is given, else name + "(x)"; without a name y=formula or y). All coefficients are exact integer, fraction or p+q*sqrt(r) strings (one radical for the whole plan). intersectionX lists EVERY x where BOTH functions are defined and equal (each once, integers, fractions and sqrt(...) allowed, at most 8); the engine and the independent observer solve it piece pair by piece pair, so a root outside either function piece interval, or at an OPEN end, is never a crossing (PIECEWISE_POLY_INTERSECTION_MISMATCH:<id>:engine finds N intersection(s) otherwise). Each crossing is a dot, or the key point it coincides with. The vertex of a quadratic reference curve, and every corner and every vertex strictly inside a piece of a piecewise one, must be listed in requiredPoints (PIECEWISE_POLY_REFERENCE_CURVE_VERTEX_NOT_IN_REQUIRED_POINTS, PIECEWISE_POLY_REFERENCE_CURVE_CORNER_NOT_IN_REQUIRED_POINTS), and a requiredPoints entry may lie on the function or on a reference curve inside that reference own domain. graphPlan.functionName (one lowercase letter, same limits) writes the header as f(x)=formula, or the name f(x) next to the curve when the function is given only by pieces. The optional graphPlan.sampleNote (at most 32 characters of digits, letters, = + - * / ( ) . and spaces, for example "a=2") writes the parameter value the figure is drawn at inside the upper left of the plot (PIECEWISE_POLY_SAMPLE_NOTE_INVALID otherwise); name the same value in the caption. A piecewise reference curve is labelled next to its own drawing (its corner), not in the band, so the two curves are not confused. A quadratic reference curve whose x squared coefficient is 0 (PIECEWISE_POLY_REFERENCE_CURVE_NOT_QUADRATIC), a curve equal to a whole piece (PIECEWISE_POLY_REFERENCE_CURVE_CONTAINS_A_PIECE), a third curve or a malformed entry (PIECEWISE_POLY_REFERENCE_CURVES_INVALID), a bad letter (PIECEWISE_POLY_FUNCTION_NAME_INVALID), more than 4 reference pieces (PIECEWISE_POLY_REFERENCE_CURVE_PIECE_COUNT_INVALID), a bad reference domain or breakpoint (PIECEWISE_POLY_REFERENCE_CURVE_SOURCE_DOMAIN_INVALID, PIECEWISE_POLY_REFERENCE_CURVE_BREAKPOINT_INVALID) or a reference formula that is not the pieces (PIECEWISE_POLY_REFERENCE_CURVE_FORMULA_MISMATCH) is refused. A cubic or quartic second curve is written as pieces:[{coefficients:[c0,c1,c2,c3(,c4)]}] (rational coefficients, at most 5 per piece; the function pieces may be cubic or quartic too, with rational coefficients above degree 2): the engine finds every root and local extremum exactly by factoring over the rationals and refuses an irreducible factor of degree 3 or more (PIECEWISE_POLY_ROOT_NOT_CLOSED_FORM, PIECEWISE_POLY_HIGH_DEGREE_IRRATIONAL_UNSUPPORTED), and every extremum of a cubic or quartic piece must be listed in requiredPoints (an inflection is not one); regions between a cubic and the x-axis, a line or a quadratic are supported; a vertical line is drawn with graphPlan.overlays (see GRAPH OVERLAYS). SHADED REGIONS: add graphPlan.regions (at most 4) to fill the band between two drawn boundaries. Each entry is {id,upper,lower,from,to,fill?,label?}: id is a short ASCII name starting with a letter; upper and lower are "f" (the function itself), "axis" (y=0), a referenceCurves id or a referenceLines id; from and to are exact integer, fraction or sqrt(...) strings (the same grammar as intersectionX) with from<to, and both boundaries must be defined over the whole closed interval (an interval end closes the band with a vertical side). The upper boundary must stay strictly above the lower one inside the interval: a boundary crossing or a zero-width touch means split it into one region per piece, while the endpoints may touch. fill is LIGHT (default) or MEDIUM (the same light greys as plane-figure regions); label is optional short math text (at most 40 characters) placed inside the band. A signed area is several regions declared side by side (one per sign piece), never one folded region. Refused with a code: GRAPH_REGION_COUNT_INVALID, GRAPH_REGION_FIELD_INVALID, GRAPH_REGION_FIELD_UNKNOWN, GRAPH_REGION_ID_INVALID, GRAPH_REGION_ID_DUPLICATE, GRAPH_REGION_BOUNDARY_UNKNOWN, GRAPH_REGION_BOUNDARY_UNDEFINED, GRAPH_REGION_DOMAIN_INVALID, GRAPH_REGION_BOUNDARIES_CROSS, GRAPH_REGION_EMPTY, GRAPH_REGION_FILL_UNSUPPORTED, GRAPH_REGION_LABEL_INVALID (graphPlan.regions belongs to piecewise-polynomial-spike-v1 only: on another family it is GRAPH_REGION_REQUIRES_PIECEWISE_POLYNOMIAL). The engine samples the exact boundary rows (the same curves the strokes draw) and paints one even-odd path beneath every stroke; the independent observer rebuilds both boundaries from the plan, checks the fill path at nine sample x values, the exact x extent, the fill colour, the paint order, that points just inside/outside are inside/outside, that regions do not overlap and that the label box lies inside the band: GRAPH_REGION_INVENTORY_MISMATCH, GRAPH_REGION_MISSING, GRAPH_REGION_UNDECLARED, GRAPH_REGION_BOUNDARY_MISMATCH, GRAPH_REGION_OUTSIDE_INTERVAL, GRAPH_REGION_OUTSIDE_BOUNDARY, GRAPH_REGION_INTERIOR_MISSING, GRAPH_REGION_FILL_MISMATCH, GRAPH_REGION_FILL_RULE_MISMATCH, GRAPH_REGION_STROKE_PRESENT, GRAPH_REGION_NOT_BENEATH_STROKES, GRAPH_REGION_LABEL_MISSING, GRAPH_REGION_LABEL_OUTSIDE, GRAPH_REGION_OVERLAP. GRAPH OVERLAYS (any single-curve graph: polynomial/quadratic/cubic-quartic, piecewise-polynomial, piecewise-affine, rational, sqrt, absolute value, exponential, logarithmic, trigonometric, exp-log pair): when the solution draws a second element ON TOP of the graph, add graphPlan.overlays. It is one general layer; every value is an exact integer, fraction or sqrt(...) string and every coordinate is recomputed exactly. overlays.lines:[{id,slope,intercept,label?}] is the straight line y=slope*x+intercept (slope may be 0) and {id,x,label?} is the vertical line x=a; each line is clipped to the plot and its equation (y=... or x=...) is written beside it (label overrides the text). overlays.points:[{id,...}] adds named points, one of: {id,curveX,curve?} a point ON the drawn curve at that x (y is computed exactly; for exp-log-pair the curve id is required), {id,intersect:[lineId,lineId]} the intersection of two overlay lines, {id,axis:"x"|"y",value} a point on an axis, or {id,x,y} an explicit point. A point id is one or two ASCII letters/digits and must not repeat a requiredPoints id; its display name comes from plan.pointNames as usual. overlays.segments:[{id,from,to,dashed?}] joins two overlay points; overlays.guides:[{id,from,axis:"x"|"y"|"BOTH"}] draws a dashed guide from a point to the axes; overlays.polygons:[{id,points:[3-6 point ids],fill?:"LIGHT"|"MEDIUM"|"NONE",label?,labelKind?:"MATH"|"TEXT"}] draws a simple closed polygon (for example the triangle OAB or the rectangle OAPB) with an optional light fill beneath the strokes; the optional label (at most 40 characters: a formula such as b for MATH, the default, or plain Korean text for TEXT) is written inside the polygon where it has the most room, and the observer refuses a label that is not wholly inside its polygon. At most 6 lines, 8 points, 6 segments, 4 guides and 2 polygons; every point and every polygon vertex must lie inside the framed viewport (the engine does not silently enlarge the frame), a point on a curve must be inside the drawn source domain, and for exp-log-pair a curve id is required on curve points. Refused with an explicit code: OVERLAY_PLAN_INVALID, OVERLAY_TOO_MANY:<kind>, OVERLAY_ID_INVALID:<kind>, OVERLAY_ID_DUPLICATE:<id>, OVERLAY_POINT_ID_CONFLICT:<id>, OVERLAY_POINT_INVALID:<id>, OVERLAY_POINT_UNKNOWN:<id>, OVERLAY_POINT_NOT_ON_CURVE:<id>, OVERLAY_POINT_OUTSIDE_SOURCE_DOMAIN:<id>, OVERLAY_POINT_OUTSIDE_VIEWPORT:<id>, OVERLAY_CURVE_REQUIRED:<id>, OVERLAY_CURVE_UNKNOWN:<id>, OVERLAY_LINE_INVALID:<id>, OVERLAY_LINE_PARALLEL:<id>, OVERLAY_LINE_OUTSIDE_VIEWPORT:<id>, OVERLAY_SEGMENT_INVALID:<id>, OVERLAY_SEGMENT_DEGENERATE:<id>, OVERLAY_GUIDE_INVALID:<id>, OVERLAY_POLYGON_INVALID:<id>, OVERLAY_POLYGON_DEGENERATE:<id>, OVERLAY_POLYGON_LABEL_INVALID:<id>, OVERLAY_POLYGON_LABEL_KIND_INVALID:<id>, OVERLAY_POLYGON_LABEL_KIND_WITHOUT_LABEL:<id>, OVERLAY_FILL_INVALID:<id>, OVERLAY_VALUE_INVALID:<id>, OVERLAY_LABEL_INVALID:<id> and OVERLAY_BASE_INVALID. The independent graph observer recomputes every overlay point, line, segment, guide, polygon and line label from the same exact inputs and reads the final SVG back; a shifted point, a moved line, a missing segment or polygon, an undeclared overlay element or a missing equation label is a FAIL. OVERLAY LABELS AND LATTICE POINTS (graphPlan.overlays, any single-curve graph): points[].coordinates:true writes the point coordinates beside it (name(x,y), for example A(-6,-3), when plan.pointNames names the point, else (x,y); integer or fraction coordinates (3/2, -1/3), a radical is OVERLAY_COORDINATES_REQUIRE_RATIONALS:<id>, OVERLAY_COORDINATES_INVALID:<id>). segments[].length:true writes the exact length of the segment (an integer or p*sqrt(q), computed from its end points; OVERLAY_SEGMENT_LENGTH_UNSUPPORTED:<id>, OVERLAY_SEGMENT_LENGTH_INVALID:<id>) beside it. points[].unnamed:true draws a dot without a name letter (an unlabelled intersection; with coordinates:true only the (x,y) form is written; OVERLAY_POINT_UNNAMED_INVALID:<id>; the observer refuses a name label for it, OVERLAY_POINT_NAME_UNEXPECTED:<id>, OVERLAY_COORDINATES_LABEL_NAMED:<id>). A point with curveX on a trigonometric graph that uses ratePi (coefficients [A,C], the rate is ratePi*pi) or phase is evaluated on that grammar. points[].labelDirection and segments[].labelDirection (N NE E SE S SW W NW) is the side the layout tries first for that label (a hint, OVERLAY_LABEL_DIRECTION_INVALID:<id>; only with coordinates or length). The equation label of a line that found no room at its first place is tried at other places on the same line (with a leader line when it has to move away); the observer reads that every line equation, coordinate label and length label belongs to its own point, line or segment (OVERLAY_LINE_LABEL_NOT_NEAR_OWNER, OVERLAY_LINE_LABEL_AMBIGUOUS_OWNER, OVERLAY_LINE_LEADER_NOT_ON_OWNER, OVERLAY_LINE_LEADER_NOT_ON_LABEL, OVERLAY_COORDINATES_LABEL_TEXT_MISMATCH, OVERLAY_COORDINATES_LABEL_AMBIGUOUS_OWNER, OVERLAY_SEGMENT_LENGTH_LABEL_TEXT_MISMATCH, OVERLAY_SEGMENT_LENGTH_LABEL_NOT_NEAR_OWNER, OVERLAY_SEGMENT_LENGTH_LABEL_AMBIGUOUS_OWNER). overlays.latticePoints (one object): {region:BELOW_CURVE or ABOVE_CURVE,xRange:[lo,hi] integers (at most 12 columns),yMin (BELOW_CURVE) or yMax (ABOVE_CURVE) an integer,strict?:true (default, the points on the curve are left out; false keeps them),counts?:true,curve? (the curves id, exp-log-pair only)} draws every integer point under (or over) the drawn curve in those columns as a small unnamed dot (at most 40; the engine counts them exactly), and counts:true writes the number of points of each column (in a row under the x-axis when the window leaves 40 px there, otherwise beside the top dot of the column). Refused with: OVERLAY_LATTICE_INVALID, OVERLAY_LATTICE_REGION_INVALID, OVERLAY_LATTICE_RANGE_INVALID, OVERLAY_LATTICE_BOUND_INVALID, OVERLAY_LATTICE_TOO_MANY, OVERLAY_LATTICE_EMPTY, OVERLAY_LATTICE_COLUMN_UNDEFINED:<x>, OVERLAY_LATTICE_POINT_COINCIDES:<x,y>, OVERLAY_POINT_OUTSIDE_VIEWPORT:lattice, OVERLAY_POINT_OUTSIDE_SOURCE_DOMAIN:lattice. The observer enumerates the integer points again from the exact curve and compares them with the dots of the final SVG one by one (OVERLAY_LATTICE_POINT_MISSING:<x,y>, OVERLAY_LATTICE_POINT_UNEXPECTED:<id>, OVERLAY_LATTICE_DOT_SIZE:<id>, OVERLAY_LATTICE_COUNT_LABEL_MISSING, OVERLAY_LATTICE_COUNT_LABEL_TEXT_MISMATCH, OVERLAY_LATTICE_COUNT_LABEL_NOT_IN_COLUMN, OVERLAY_LATTICE_COUNT_LABEL_AMBIGUOUS_OWNER). DISPLAY WINDOW (rational-spike-v1, inverse proportion y=k/(d0+d1*x) only): graphPlan.displayWindow:[xmin,xmax,ymin,ymax] makes the frame exactly that window at one scale on both axes (448 px wide, as tall as its aspect needs; the y range is widened by less than a pixel to a whole pixel height) instead of the automatic window around the pole; the curve is clipped to it and a branch or asymptote outside it is not drawn. The origin must be inside (DISPLAY_WINDOW_MUST_CONTAIN_ORIGIN); a window under 160 px (DISPLAY_WINDOW_TOO_FLAT) or over 640 px (DISPLAY_WINDOW_TOO_TALL) tall, a pole exactly on its edge (DISPLAY_WINDOW_POLE_ON_EDGE), a window with no curve in it (DISPLAY_WINDOW_CURVE_NOT_VISIBLE), a malformed list (DISPLAY_WINDOW_INVALID), axisSqueeze (DISPLAY_WINDOW_FORBIDS_AXIS_SQUEEZE), a required point outside (REQUIRED_GRAPH_POINT_OUTSIDE_DISPLAY_WINDOW:<id>), another family (DISPLAY_WINDOW_UNSUPPORTED_FAMILY) or a rational that is not an inverse proportion (DISPLAY_WINDOW_REQUIRES_INVERSE_PROPORTION) is refused; the engine never widens the window (an overlay point or line outside it is OVERLAY_POINT_OUTSIDE_VIEWPORT:<id>). Without axisTickValues the ticks are a 1/2/5 grid with at most 8 labels per axis, and formulaLabel goes where the window is emptiest near its curve (28 to 120 px from it); graphPlan.formulaAt:[x,y] (inside the window, only with displayWindow) names the spot yourself (FORMULA_AT_INVALID, FORMULA_AT_OUTSIDE_WINDOW, FORMULA_AT_REQUIRES_DISPLAY_WINDOW, FORMULA_AT_UNSUPPORTED_FAMILY; the observer reads that the label stands within 110 px of it, FORMULA_LABEL_NOT_AT_ANCHOR). In such a plan the line equation labels are placed under the line-owned rule from the start, like the labels of coordinates, lengths and lattice points. The independent observer re-validates the window against the viewport (DISPLAY_WINDOW_VIEWPORT_MISMATCH) and recomputes from the exact coefficients which branches the window must show (RATIONAL_WINDOW_BRANCH_MISSING:<left|right>, RATIONAL_WINDOW_BRANCH_UNEXPECTED:<left|right>, RATIONAL_WINDOW_SHOWS_NO_CURVE). AXIS TITLES (any single-curve graph): graphPlan.axisTitles:{x?,y?} writes a short Korean title with its unit (at most 10 characters of Korean letters, ASCII letters, digits, space and ()/.,%-, for example 시간(분) or 거리(km)) at the end of that drawn axis instead of the letter x or y, as plain text that must find room (never dropped). Refused with AXIS_TITLES_INVALID (not an object with x and/or y), AXIS_TITLE_INVALID:<axis> (empty, padded, longer than 10 characters or any other character, TeX included), AXIS_TITLE_AXIS_HIDDEN:<axis> (that axis is not drawn). The observer reads the title from the final SVG (AXIS_TITLE_MISSING, AXIS_TITLE_TEXT_MISMATCH, AXIS_TITLE_NOT_AT_AXIS_END). SOLID FIGURES AND NETS (solid-figure-v1, middle school): when the stored solution needs a prism, pyramid, pyramid frustum, cylinder, cone, cone frustum, sphere or hemisphere drawn as a solid (hidden edges dashed, vertex names, lengths beside their edges), or the net of a prism, pyramid, cylinder or cone, use {capability:"solid-figure-v1",unit?:"cm",solids:[1 to 3 solids]|net:{...},dims?,highlights?,segments?,section?,solidCaption?:{text},caption,sourceConditions,newVisualInformation}. A solid is {kind:"PRISM",base,height,names?} (base {kind:"RECT",width,depth}|{kind:"REGULAR",n:3..8,side}|{kind:"POLYGON",points:[[u,v],...],plane?:"XY"|"XZ"} with at most 8 simple points; plane XZ makes the polygon a front face extruded by height along y, for a cuboid with a slanted cut), {kind:"PYRAMID",base,height,apex?:[x,y]} (convex base), {kind:"FRUSTUM",base,height,topScale:0..1,foot?}, {kind:"CYLINDER",radius,height}, {kind:"CONE",radius,height,orientation?}, {kind:"CONE_FRUSTUM",radius,topRadius,height,orientation?}, {kind:"SPHERE",radius}, {kind:"HEMISPHERE",radius}, {kind:"REGULAR_POLYHEDRON",faces:4|8|12|20,side} (the regular tetrahedron, octahedron, dodecahedron or icosahedron, every edge side long, standing on the floor; vertices in model order, faces f0, f1, ...; a cube is a PRISM over a RECT base) and {kind:"BIPYRAMID",base (convex),height} (two pyramids of that height on one base, vertices ring then upper apex then lower apex, faces top0.. and bottom0..; the apexes are #apex and #apex2); all lengths are real positive numbers and the figure is drawn to scale in one fixed projection. orientation is "APEX_UP" (default) or "APEX_DOWN": an apex-down cone/cone frustum is a vessel whose point (narrow end) rests on the base plane and whose wide circle is the rim at height h; radius is always the wide radius. A round solid may take inside:"s1" to sit on a cylinder s1's axis (a sphere, cone or cone frustum that fits inside it; a sphere inscribed in a cylinder of height 2r is the usual case).
