A SPHERE may take cut:{kind:"HALF"|"QUARTER"|"OCTANT"|"THREE_QUARTERS"|"SEVEN_EIGHTHS",facing?:"CUT"|"CURVED"}: the sphere cut by planes through its centre, only the piece that is left being drawn (a half with its flat disk, a quarter wedge between two perpendicular planes with its two half-disks, one eighth with its three quarter-disks, the sphere with a quarter wedge or one eighth taken out); the flat cut faces that face the viewer are shaded, edges behind the piece are dashed, a named centre (names:{center:"O"}) gets a dot, and dims:[{radius:"center"}] draws the radius along a half axis that is seen. facing CUT (default) turns the cut faces toward the viewer, CURVED turns the piece about its centre so that the curved surface faces the viewer and the cut faces are behind (a radius dim then has no seen radius and is refused); two pieces shown apart (a sphere cut in two) are two HALF solids side by side, and every piece keeps its own solid id for dims. cut is only for a SPHERE and does not combine with inside or count. names is a string of capital letters in the order base then top (or apex) ("ABCDEFGH", "." leaves a vertex unnamed) or {base,top,apex} (round solids {center,top,apex}); a list entry such as "M,A,I" (2 to 4 names) gives one polyhedron vertex several names, written as one label and usable in segments, dims and marks (a letters string ignores commas); without names the letters run A,B,C,... and a name is used once in the figure. A source figure that shows no vertex names keeps them unnamed (names all ".") and writes a vertex as a reference wherever a name is written (segments from/to, dims edge/from/to, highlights polygon, marks at/refs/segments): "#b0" the i-th vertex of the base ring (0-based, in the order of the base polygon), "#t2" the i-th of the top ring of a prism or frustum, "#apex" the apex of a pyramid, "#v5" the i-th vertex in model order (counting number k is #v(k-1)); with several polyhedra the solid id comes first ("s2#b0"), and a dims edge may be a list of two references ["#b1","#t1"]; a round solid or a solid with cuts takes no references. count:{vertices?:true,edges?:true} on a prism, pyramid or frustum writes counting numbers for a counting question: 1, 2, ... beside the vertices (in the order base ring then top ring or apex; the vertices must then be unnamed, names "........") and the circled numbers beside every edge midpoint, hidden edges too (around the base ring, up the sides, around the top ring; at most 20 edges); faces cannot be counted. dims draws a length beside its segment: {edge:"AB"} or {from,to} (a drawn edge or a segment of segments), {radius:"bottom"|"top"|"center"|"level"}, {height:true} (a pyramid, frustum, cylinder, cone), {slant:true} (cone, cone frustum), {level:true|"bottom"} (the height up to the LEVEL section) or {level:"top"} (the height from the LEVEL section up to the apex or top); an optional label (TeX) must equal the length when it is a number, otherwise it is a symbol such as r, h or 2r (symbols may use TeX commands such as the one for pi, written with one backslash); the engine writes exact lengths itself (5, 7.5, the square root form 5 times root 2, a multiple of pi) with the unit. cuts:[{through:[["AD","1/2","M"],["BC","1/2","N"],["CG","1/2","P"],["DH","1/2","Q"]],keep?:"F"}] on a PRISM, PYRAMID or FRUSTUM (one or two planes, one after the other, convex solids only) draws the solid that remains after a plane cuts a part off: through lists points, each [edge written as its two vertex names, "p/q" the fraction of the edge from the first named vertex (0<p/q<1), optional name of the new vertex ("." for none)], and vertices:["A","C","F"] lists corners of the solid (names, or in the first plane references "#b0" "#t2" "#apex" "#v5"), 3 to 6 points in all, in one plane (the plane through three corners next to B cuts the corner B off with no new vertex); the side with more vertices stays (equal numbers need keep, a vertex name that stays), a vertex on the plane stays, every edge crossing the plane gets a new vertex and the section becomes a face; names of removed vertices cannot be used afterwards, the second plane names edges of the solid left by the first, count and a height dim are not allowed with cuts. highlights:[{polygon:["E","G","C"]}] shades a planar polygon of drawn segments, and highlights:[{face:"side1",fill:"WARM"},{face:"top"},{face:"bottom",solid:"s1"}] shades a whole face of a prism, pyramid or frustum by its face name (bottom, top, side0, side1, ...: side i stands on the i-th edge of the base; no vertex names are needed, so a figure with unnamed vertices can be shaded; solid names the polyhedron when there are several; a hidden face is shaded through the faces in front of it; not on a solid with cuts or on a round solid), fill being LIGHT (default), MEDIUM, WARM, GREEN, ROSE or VIOLET, also on the polygon form, at most 8 highlights, an unknown face is SOLID_HIGHLIGHT_FACE_UNKNOWN and a bad tint SOLID_HIGHLIGHT_FILL_INVALID; segments:[{from,to,style:"SOLID"|"DASHED"|"AUTO",role:"REF"|"ACCENT"}] draws an extra segment (a diagonal, or an edge to emphasize; AUTO dashes it when the solid hides it). section:{kind:"AXIAL"} shades the plane through the axis of a cylinder, cone or frustum; section:{kind:"LEVEL",at:"1/3"} also cuts a whole sphere or a hemisphere (not nested, not cut) in the circle of radius sqrt(R^2-(z-zc)^2) at that fraction of its height (no AXIAL, fill or level dims there); it draws the horizontal plane at that fraction of the height and, on an apex-down vessel, fills the smaller similar vessel below the level and draws its two inner sides; on an apex-up cone, frustum or cylinder section:{kind:"LEVEL",at:"1/2",fill:"BOTTOM"|"TOP"} shades the body below the plane (a frustum cut off a cone) or above it (the small cone), its outline being the hull of the circles that bound that part (a fill on an apex-down vessel is SOLID_SECTION_FILL_UNSUPPORTED:APEX_DOWN, another value SOLID_SECTION_FILL_INVALID). marks:{rightAngles:[{at:"G",refs:["E","C"]}],equalLengths:[{count:1,segments:["AB","CD"]}],angles:[{refs:["B","A","D"],value?,arcs:1..3,equalGroup?}],equalSlant?:true|{solid?,count?}|[...]} draws right-angle squares, equal-length ticks and angle arcs on named points (equalSlant puts the same ticks on the left and the right slant of a cone or cone frustum, no names needed; its tick counts differ from those of every other group); the plan asserts a fact the engine checks in 3D (the two rays of a right angle are truly perpendicular, the equal group's segments truly have one length, the angle matches value within 0.5 degree) and both rays/segments must be drawn edges or segments; equalLengths groups have distinct counts 1..3 and equalGroup angles have one size and arc count. A net is {solid:{PRISM|PYRAMID|FRUSTUM},root:faceId,joins:[[parentFace,childFace],...],corners?:{faceId:[names]},dims?:[{face,edge,label?}],highlights?:[{face,fill?:"LIGHT"|"MEDIUM"}],segments?:[{face,edge,role?}|{face,diagonal:[i,j],role?}|{from,to,role?}]} with faces named bottom, top, side0, side1, ... (side i stands on base edge i); joins form a spanning tree of the faces (adjacent faces only) and the engine unfolds along it, refusing overlaps; highlights tints whole faces of the net and segments stresses one drawn edge in role "ACCENT" (red, default) or "REF" (bold black), either as the edge-th side of a face (cycle order, like dims) or as the two corner names written on the net (the same two names at two different drawn edges is refused; a fold written from both faces is one edge); {face,diagonal:[i,j]} (or the two corner names when no drawn edge carries them) draws and stresses the diagonal between two non-neighbouring corners i and j of one face, such as a diagonal the original net shows; a polyhedron net only; or {kind:"CYLINDER",radius,height,dims?:[{part:"rect-width"|"rect-height"|"radius-top"|"radius-bottom",label?}]}, or {kind:"CONE",radius,slant|height,dims?:[{part:"slant"|"arc"|"radius"|"angle",label?}]}. Any net may also carry faceLabels?:[{face,text?}] (one label in the middle of each listed face, wholly inside it: the exact area with the unit squared when text is omitted, a numeric text must equal that area, any other text is a symbol, or a face symbol that only numbers or names the face: one circled number (①-⑳), circled Hangul letter, Hangul letter or jamo (가, ㄴ), or such a letter in parentheses ((나)), no area is checked for it; a cylinder net has the faces rect, bottom and top, a cone net sector and base), a polyhedron net rightAngles?:[{face,corner}] (a small square at that corner of the face, counted in the face's cycle order, where the two edges are truly perpendicular; SOLID_NET_RIGHT_ANGLE_NOT_RIGHT lists the right corners), and a cylinder or cone net excluded?:["bottom","top"] or ["base"] (those circles are not part of the surface: drawn dashed and grey). A polyhedron net may carry edgeLabels?:[{face,edge,text?}] (at most 10, numbered after dims: no text writes the exact edge length, a TeX text a number that must equal it or a symbol, a text with Hangul (at most 12 characters, 밑변) a word that only names the edge; edge counts in the face's cycle order like dims). A rotation solid (CYLINDER, CONE, CONE_FRUSTUM, not nested) may carry axis:true or axis:{label:"회전축"}: its axis is drawn as a dash-dot line 14 percent of the height beyond both ends, with the word beside it. Any figure may carry labels:[{text,at}] (at most 6; text is a short Korean/English word of at most 12 characters copied from the source figure, never TeX; at is a vertex name or reference, {axis:true}, {slant:true}, {edge:[a,b]} or {face:"side0"} (a visible face of an uncut polyhedron), plus solid when several solids; one word per target; a net takes none). A figure needs at least one name, length or word (a face label counts), or something stressed (a shaded face, segments, a section, marks, a cut, an axis, or in a net a shaded face, a stressed edge, a right angle, an excluded circle), or a solidCaption, or to be a panel of a composite grid (the panel caption carries the text); only a bare, uncaptioned figure is SOLID_FIGURE_NEEDS_A_LABEL. segments takes at most 10 entries. The same solid can be shown in 2-4 panels with a different group of segments emphasized in each, and a solid can stand beside a plane figure: put each figure as a panel of composite-panels-v1 (see the multi-panel contract), a solid panel being a solid-figure-v1 plan (solids or net) without caption, solidCaption, sourceConditions, newVisualInformation or sourceQuote. A solid-figure-v1 plan may carry sourceQuote (a string copied from the item): it is provenance only and never drawn (a bad one is SOLID_SOURCE_QUOTE_INVALID). Not supported (answer UNSUPPORTED): a net, cuts or face counting numbers of a regular polyhedron or bipyramid, more than one non-sphere solid nested in a cylinder (several balls are fine: see stack), more than 3 solids, a solid nested in anything but a cylinder (a cube inside a sphere), a solid nested in a nested solid, cutting a cylinder, cone or hemisphere by a plane, cutting a sphere by a plane that misses its centre or into other pieces than the five named ones, an explode flag on a cut, more than two planes (cuts), a flat bowl, perspective views, marks with labels or parallel marks, and a name or length that finds no room (SOLID_LABELS_NO_ROOM: use fewer labels). Refusals: SOLID_PLAN_INVALID, SOLID_KIND_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_ORIENTATION_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_NEST_INVALID, SOLID_NEST_UNKNOWN, SOLID_NEST_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_NEST_DOES_NOT_FIT, SOLID_NEST_CHAIN, SOLID_MARKS_INVALID, SOLID_MARK_ID_INVALID, SOLID_MARK_UNKNOWN, SOLID_MARK_RAY_NOT_DRAWN, SOLID_MARK_SEGMENT_NOT_DRAWN, SOLID_MARK_NOT_RIGHT_ANGLE, SOLID_MARK_LENGTH_MISMATCH, SOLID_MARK_GROUP_AMBIGUOUS, SOLID_MARK_GROUP_ARC_COUNT, SOLID_MARK_GROUP_MISMATCH, SOLID_MARK_ANGLE_MISMATCH, SOLID_MARK_ANGLE_DEGENERATE, SOLID_LEVEL_APEX_HIDDEN, SOLID_PARAM_INVALID, SOLID_BASE_INVALID, SOLID_BASE_NOT_SIMPLE, SOLID_BASE_NOT_CONVEX, SOLID_NAMES_INVALID, SOLID_NAME_DUPLICATE, SOLID_NAME_UNKNOWN, SOLID_DIMS_INVALID, SOLID_DIM_SEGMENT_NOT_DRAWN, SOLID_DIM_VALUE_MISMATCH, SOLID_DIM_NOT_EXPRESSIBLE, SOLID_HIGHLIGHT_NOT_PLANAR, SOLID_SECTION_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_NET_NOT_A_TREE, SOLID_NET_FACES_NOT_ADJACENT, SOLID_NET_FACES_OVERLAP, SOLID_COUNT_REQUEST_INVALID, SOLID_COUNT_FACES_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_COUNT_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_COUNT_NAMES_CONFLICT, SOLID_COUNT_TOO_MANY, SOLID_NET_HIGHLIGHT_INVALID, SOLID_NET_SEGMENT_INVALID, SOLID_NET_SEGMENT_DUPLICATE, SOLID_NET_EDGE_UNKNOWN, SOLID_NET_EDGE_AMBIGUOUS, SOLID_NET_EMPHASIS_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_FIGURE_NEEDS_A_LABEL, SOLID_SEGMENTS_INVALID, SOLID_SOURCE_QUOTE_INVALID, SOLID_CUT_COUNT_INVALID, SOLID_CUT_INVALID, SOLID_CUT_EDGE_UNKNOWN, SOLID_CUT_NOT_PLANAR, SOLID_CUT_DEGENERATE, SOLID_CUT_NO_EFFECT, SOLID_CUT_KEEP_AMBIGUOUS, SOLID_CUT_KEEP_INVALID, SOLID_CUT_NOT_CONVEX, SOLID_CUT_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_SPHERE_CUT_INVALID, SOLID_SPHERE_CUT_KIND_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_SPHERE_CUT_FACING_INVALID, SOLID_SPHERE_CUT_OFF_CENTRE, SOLID_SPHERE_CUT_EXPLODE_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_SPHERE_CUT_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_SPHERE_CUT_NEST_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_SPHERE_CUT_RADIUS_HIDDEN, SOLID_HIGHLIGHT_INVALID, SOLID_HIGHLIGHT_FACE_UNKNOWN, SOLID_HIGHLIGHT_FACE_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_HIGHLIGHT_FILL_INVALID, SOLID_SECTION_FILL_INVALID, SOLID_SECTION_FILL_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_AXIS_INVALID, SOLID_AXIS_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_LABELS_INVALID, SOLID_LABEL_TEXT_INVALID, SOLID_LABEL_AT_INVALID, SOLID_LABEL_AT_UNKNOWN, SOLID_LABEL_EDGE_NOT_DRAWN, SOLID_LABEL_FACE_UNKNOWN, SOLID_LABEL_FACE_HIDDEN, SOLID_LABEL_FACE_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_LABEL_AXIS_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_LABEL_SLANT_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_LABEL_DUPLICATE_TARGET, SOLID_LABELS_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_EDGE_LABEL_INVALID, SOLID_EDGE_LABEL_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_MARK_SLANT_UNSUPPORTED, SOLID_CUT_VERTEX_UNKNOWN, SOLID_CUT_VERTEX_REF_UNSUPPORTED. SET DIAGRAMS (set-diagram-figure-v1): when the stored solution needs a Venn diagram or a finite mapping diagram, use {capability:"set-diagram-figure-v1",diagram:{kind:"VENN"|"MAPPING"|"MEMBERSHIP_TABLE",...},caption,sourceConditions,newVisualInformation}; the engine derives every region, the truth of the set expression on each atomic cell, the shading geometry and the placement of each element itself, and an independent observer reads the final SVG back. VENN: sets is 1 to 3 entries {id:"A",name?} (one capital letter, not U), each with at most 12 elements [{text:"2",in:["A","B"],math?}] where in lists exactly the sets the element belongs to (empty in = outside every set); an element whose sets cannot occur in the drawn layout is refused (SET_ELEMENT_REGION_EMPTY), and a text that looks like an interval or an inequality is refused (SET_ELEMENT_TEXT_UNSUPPORTED). shade is a set expression over U and the set ids with union, intersection, difference and a postfix complement (for example "(A∩B)∪(A∪B)^c" or "A-B"); U itself may be written. An expression true on no cell is refused (SET_SHADE_EMPTY). For the standard inclusion B⊂A of two sets add subset:["B","A"] (inner first); the inner set is drawn wholly inside the outer one and only the nonempty cells are used. MAPPING: columns is 2 or 3 entries {id:"X",name?,elements:["1","2","3"]} with 1 to 8 distinct short element texts per column (the column id is a capital letter with an optional digit, the name defaults to the id; a self-map uses two columns with the same name and different ids). arrows is 1 to 24 entries {from:{column:0,element:0},to:{column:1,element:1},style?:"SOLID"|"DASHED"}; an arrow joins two adjacent columns in either direction (X->Y and Y->X both, so a function and its reverse can share one figure; X->Y->Z is also supported), no duplicate pair and no arrow between distant columns (MAPPING_ARROW_DIRECTION_UNSUPPORTED). Column and element indices are zero-based positions in the plan. highlight is optional: {arrows:[arrow indices]} thickens those arrows and {elements:[{column,element}]} puts a light box behind those elements. MEMBERSHIP_TABLE is the faithful figure when a finite universe is given with two to four sets and overlapping circles cannot show every membership combination: sets is 2 to 4 entries {id,name?} (one capital letter, not U), elements is 1 to 12 rows [{text,in,math?}] where in lists exactly the sets that contain the element, and element texts must be distinct. The engine draws one row per element, one column per set, and an exact membership mark in every cell the plan declares; it never adds or drops a mark. More than 3 sets for VENN, more than 4 sets for MEMBERSHIP_TABLE, more than 3 columns, an element text that is not a short value, more than 8 elements in a column, and more than 12 elements in a Venn diagram or membership table are refused (SET_COUNT_UNSUPPORTED, MAPPING_COLUMN_COUNT_UNSUPPORTED, MAPPING_TOO_MANY_ELEMENTS, SET_TOO_MANY_ELEMENTS). Sets written symbolically or as intervals (for example {x|x<=10}) are not Venn elements: draw only the finite elements the problem puts in the regions, or answer UNSUPPORTED. The observer checks by sampling the final shading path points against the expression, by recomputing each element label box membership from the drawn ellipses, and by tracing each arrow from its source label box to its target label box; an element label that does not fit inside its region is refused (SET_ELEMENT_NO_ROOM, SET_LABEL_NO_ROOM, SET_DIAGRAM_RENDERED_LAYOUT_FAIL) so draw fewer elements or fewer sets in one figure. MULTI-CURVE FIGURE (multi-curve-figure-v1): when one source figure genuinely needs THREE OR MORE distinct nonlinear curves at once (not two curves, not two curves plus a line, not one curve split into branches), use {capability:"multi-curve-figure-v1",graphPlan:{family:"multi-curve",curves:[...],axisTickValues:{x:[],y:[]}},caption,sourceConditions,newVisualInformation}. curves has 3 to 6 entries; each is {id,kind:"Y_OF_X"|"X_OF_Y",coefficients:[c0,c1,c2],domain:[lo,hi]} plus branch:"BOTH"|"POSITIVE"|"NEGATIVE" for X_OF_Y (default BOTH) and an optional label, an exact ASCII math string such as y=2-x^2 that is drawn beside that curve with the existing math-label machinery (bad text is MULTI_CURVE_LABEL_INVALID:<id>). graphPlan may also carry the same overlays layer as the other graph families (lines, points, segments, guides, polygons; every coordinate must lie inside the framed viewport and the frame is never silently enlarged) and axisTickValues may place exact unit ticks; when no point name, equation label or other counted label exists, give at least one exact axis tick value so the publication text gate has real text. Y_OF_X draws y=c0+c1*x+c2*x^2 on the closed x interval; X_OF_Y draws x=c0+c1*y+c2*y^2 on the closed y interval as its upper (POSITIVE) and/or lower (NEGATIVE) branch, which the engine converts to y(x) exactly. Every coefficient and end is an exact integer or fraction string (or one p+q*sqrt(r)); c2 must be nonzero. The engine frames all curves at one scale (vertical squeeze at most 1/2), gives every branch its own exact draw interval and tags it CURVE_<id> or CURVE_<id>_POS/_NEG, and the independent observer recomputes every curve and branch from the same coefficients, compares the frozen inventory, and reads the final SVG: a shifted point, a curve outside its interval or branch side, a wrong endpoint, a wrong interior arc or a missing/extra curve is a FAIL. Refused with an explicit code: MULTI_CURVE_COUNT_INVALID (fewer than 3 or more than 6), MULTI_CURVE_FIELD_UNKNOWN:<field>, MULTI_CURVE_CURVE_INVALID:<id>, MULTI_CURVE_ID_INVALID, MULTI_CURVE_KIND_INVALID:<id>, MULTI_CURVE_COEFFICIENT_INVALID:<id>, MULTI_CURVE_NOT_QUADRATIC:<id>, MULTI_CURVE_DOMAIN_INVALID:<id>, MULTI_CURVE_BRANCH_INVALID:<id>, MULTI_CURVE_BRANCH_EMPTY:<id>, MULTI_CURVE_LABEL_INVALID:<id>, MULTI_CURVE_FRAME_DEGENERATE, MULTI_CURVE_TOO_NARROW. This family is for three or more distinct curves; a single curve, two curves, two curves plus a line, or a curve with piecewise branches uses the existing family instead. CONTAINER FILL FIGURES (container-fill-figure-v1): when the stored solution reads a vessel that is filled with water at a constant rate against the graph of the water height over time (matching vessels with their height-time graphs, choosing the graph of one vessel, explaining why a graph is steep or flat), use {capability:"container-fill-figure-v1",container:{pairs:[{vessel:{name?,profile:[...]},graphName?,segmentNotes?,flowPi?,xTicks?,yTicks?}],guides?,xTitle?,yTitle?,origin?},caption,sourceConditions,newVisualInformation}. The engine draws every vessel as its cross-section and computes the height-time graph itself (the time to reach a height is proportional to the volume below it, exact at every piece boundary, sampled densely inside a frustum): never describe or draw the curve yourself. profile is the vessel from the bottom up, 1 to 4 pieces on one vertical axis: {kind:"CYLINDER",radius,height} or {kind:"FRUSTUM",r0 (bottom radius),r1 (top radius),height}, all exact positive strings; r0 "0" is a cone with its point down and is allowed only for the first piece; the bottom radius of a piece may differ from the top radius of the piece below (a wide cylinder under a thin tube draws a step). pairs holds 1 to 3 rows, each a vessel with its own graph on one vertical scale; name (the vessel, such as "A" or "물컵") and graphName (such as "ㄱ" or the circled "㉠" the problem prints) are optional, at most 6 characters, and stand under the vessel and under the graph. Give the proportions of the picture (a cup that widens upward: r0 2, r1 4), not measured values the problem does not state. guides true draws a dotted level guide from the vessel at every piece boundary and at the top to its kink in the graph. segmentNotes [{segment (0 is the bottom piece),text (at most 16 characters)}] put a short Korean note beside that piece of the curve. xTitle and yTitle (at most 14 characters, defaults "시간" and "높이"; write units yourself, for example "시간(초)" and "높이(cm)"); origin false hides the O. Numeric ticks are drawn only where you give a value: yTicks (heights, increasing, at most the vessel height) and, when the problem states the inflow, flowPi (the water volume per time unit as a multiple of pi, for example "9" for 9 pi cubic cm per second) with xTicks (times, increasing, at most the total time). Never write the choice number the problem asks for. Refused with a code: more than 3 pairs or none (CONTAINER_PAIR_COUNT_INVALID), a malformed profile or more than 4 pieces (CONTAINER_PROFILE_INVALID), a piece kind other than CYLINDER or FRUSTUM (CONTAINER_PIECE_KIND_UNSUPPORTED), a sphere, hemisphere or other curved wall (CONTAINER_CURVED_PROFILE_UNSUPPORTED), a tilted, off-axis or lopsided vessel (CONTAINER_ASYMMETRIC_UNSUPPORTED), a pointed top or a cone point above the first piece (CONTAINER_APEX_UNSUPPORTED), a radius or height that is not a positive exact number (CONTAINER_DIMENSION_INVALID), xTicks without flowPi (CONTAINER_XTICKS_NEED_FLOW), proportions that cannot be drawn legibly (CONTAINER_PROPORTION_UNSUPPORTED: a piece under 12 px high, a part narrower than 6 px, two kinks under 8 px apart), a note with no room (CONTAINER_NOTE_NO_ROOM) or a label that cannot be placed (CONTAINER_LABEL_OUT_OF_BOUNDS, CONTAINER_LABELS_TOO_CLOSE, CONTAINER_LABEL_ON_LINE). A hemispherical or spherical vessel, an inflow that changes with time and a hand-drawn graph are not supported: answer UNSUPPORTED. LATTICE PATH FIGURES (lattice-path-figure-v1): when the stored solution counts shortest paths on a rectangular road network (grid of squares) from A to B, with roads that must not be used (forbidden) or must be used (via) or one example path, use {capability:"lattice-path-figure-v1",lattice:{grid:{cols,rows},points:{A:[0,0],B:[6,3],P:[4,1]},from?:"A",to?:"B",forbidden?:[["P","Q"]],via?:[[p,q]],counts?:true|false|[names],hideTargetCount?:true,highlightPath?:[names or [x,y]]},caption,sourceConditions,newVisualInformation}; cols 1-10, rows 1-8, [0,0] is the lower left crossing, a segment joins two neighbouring crossings (a name or [x,y]), forbidden roads are drawn broken and via roads bold. counts:true makes the engine count the shortest paths (moving only toward the target) from from to each crossing that can still be completed into a valid path and write those small numbers; the final count at the target (the answer) is hidden unless hideTargetCount is false, so never write counts values yourself. highlightPath is one example path (neighbouring crossings, no repeated or forbidden road; moving back is allowed and then no count is meaningful). A non-rectangular road network, diagonal roads or a count of paths that go back are UNSUPPORTED. An independent observer walks every valid path by brute force and reads the final SVG back (LATTICE_*, LP_* codes).
~~~~
